          THE CITY OF ST. PETERSBURG ORDAINS THE FOLLOWING:

SECTION 1—FINDINGS: The City Council of the City of St. Petersburg, Florida, (“City
Council”) hereby makes the following findings:

(A)       In the mid-1970s, the City began construction of a multi-family affordable housing com-
          plex currently known as “Jamestown Apartments and Townhomes” (“Jamestown”).
          Immediately to the north of Jamestown is a City park (“Unity Park”) that is adjacent to
          park-like, recreational open space within the Jamestown complex (the “Jamestown Open
          Space”).

(B)       On June 17, 1982, City Council adopted ordinance 582-F for the purpose of adopting a
          document entitled “Detailed Community Redevelopment Plan—Jamestown Project Area”
          as a community redevelopment plan under Florida Statutes chapter 163 (as amended, the
          “1982 Plan”) for an area generally described as “immediately west of the Central Business
          District and bounded by 9th and 16th Streets North and Arlington and 4th Avenues North.”

(C)       The area covered by the 1982 Plan was subdivided into four smaller areas, which were
          designated as phases I, IIA, IIB, and III. Only the areas covered by phases I and IIA are
          relevant to this ordinance, and the use of those areas can be generally described as follows:


00898861 / v03                                   1 of 7                            Jamestown / City Infill Lots
          (1)    The area covered by phase I is used for Unity Park, Jamestown, and the Jamestown
                 Open Space.

          (2)    The area covered by phase IIA is used primarily for single- and multi-family resi-
                 dential housing immediately west of Jamestown. It also includes nine City-owned
                 parcels (the “City Infill Lots”). Four of the City Infill Lots are immediately north
                 of the residential parcels, adjacent to 4th Avenue North, and the remaining five City
                 Infill Lots are located immediately south of the residential parcels and immediately
                 north of six commercial parcels off Burlington Avenue North.

(D)       The 1982 Plan stated that the residential parcels in phase IIA should be “buffered from the
          surrounding major streets and land uses as much as possible” and that lots should be ac-
          quired by the City for the “development of a public open space buffer system.” The City
          Infill Lots were acquired by the City to further those elements of the 1982 Plan.

(E)       On November 6, 1984, in a City-wide referendum held pursuant to ordinance 780-F, voters
          approved an amendment to the City Charter that incorporated into section 1.02 a map of
          City-owned park and waterfront property (the “Charter Map”) for the purpose of (i) con-
          clusively determining which parcels owned by the City at that time were considered park
          or waterfront property and (ii) establishing limitations on the use and disposition of those
          parcels.

(F)       As shown in the excerpt below, the Charter Map included a composite parcel that is com-
          posed of Unity Park, the Jamestown Open Space, and the City Infill Lots. It was designated
          as parcel number 33 and identified on the key as “Jamestown.”




00898861 / v03                                   2 of 7                           Jamestown / City Infill Lots
(G)       The visual depiction on the Charter Map was supplemented by the following legal descrip-
          tion of that parcel, which was incorporated by reference into the Charter Map as “legal
          footnote” number 9:

                 Lot 1, Block 1, Jamestown Replat Unit Three. Land designated as
                 “Public Park” in Jamestown Replat Unit One. Lot 1, Block 1,
                 Jamestown Replat Unit Four. Lots 1 and 12, Block F, Harvey’s
                 Subdivision. Lots 1, 2, 11 and 12, Block G, Harvey’s Subdivision.
                 Lots 17 and 18, Block I, Harvey’s Subdivision. W 45 ft., more or
                 less, of Lots 13 and 14. All of Lots 15, 16 and 20, Block J, Harvey’s
                 Subdivision. Lots 12, 13, and 21, Block K, Harvey’s Subdivision.
                 Lot 12, Block L, Harvey’s Subdivision.

(H)       Although the Charter Map grouped Unity Park, the Jamestown Open Space, and the City
          Infill Lots into a single composite “park” parcel, that grouping is inconsistent with historic
          and current use:

          (1)    Unity Park is maintained to landscaping standards for City parks and open to the
                 public.

          (2)    Similarly, the adjacent Jamestown Open Space is maintained in a manner similar to
                 a park and is open to Jamestown residents for recreational purposes.

          (3)    In contrast, the nine City Infill Lots are fragmented by housing, streets, and alleys;
                 maintained only as necessary to comply with City Code standards for a vacant lot;
                 and posted with signage declaring each lot to be off-limits to the public.

(I)       Accordingly, the City Infill Lots do not function as a City park or serve a park-like purpose.

(J)       Additionally, the City Infill Lots are not needed for the “buffer” purposes contemplated by
          the 1982 Plan, which expired on June 17, 2012, after the 30-year period provided by the
          plan had been completed.

(K)       After sitting vacant for more than 40 years, the City Infill Lots should be integrated into
          the adjacent neighborhood for affordable or workforce housing purposes. According to
          analysis by City staff:

          (1)    Four of the parcels are approximately 5,000 square feet and would be appropriate
                 for single-family housing. Housing on those parcels can be developed through the
                 Affordable Lot Disposition Program without further action of City Council.




00898861 / v03                                    3 of 7                            Jamestown / City Infill Lots
          (2)    The remaining five parcels are approximately 10,000 or 13,000 square feet each
                 and would be appropriate for multi-family housing. Development of affordable or
                 workforce housing on those parcels would occur through a request for proposals
                 from developers that could include one or more parcels (i.e., a single developer
                 could propose simultaneous development of multiple parcels). Acceptance of those
                 proposals would require City Council approval.

          (3)    The current zoning and future land use map designations for the City Infill Lots are
                 Neighborhood Suburban Estate (NS-E) and Recreation/Open Space (R/OS). If
                 those designations are changed to match the current designations for the adjacent
                 residential parcels, which are Neighborhood Suburban Multifamily-2 (NSM-2) and
                 Residential High (RH), the City Infill Lots could be used to provide 41 units of
                 housing—4 single-family units and 37 multi-family units.

(L)       Before the City Infill Lots can be used for housing purposes in the manner described in the
          preceding findings, the City Charter must be amended to remove the City Infill Lots from
          the Charter Map. Following such an amendment, the City Infill Lots would no longer be
          classified as parkland under the City Charter, which would allow the City to use the City
          Infill Lots in accordance with applicable zoning and land use map designations. The spe-
          cific parcels that would be removed by the amendment are identified with yellow shading
          on the diagram attached to this ordinance exhibit A.

(M)       The applicable legal footnote for the “Jamestown” parcel contains a reference to Lot 12,
          Block L, Harvey’s Subdivision, which has never been owned by the City and appears to
          have been added to that footnote by mistake. Accordingly, the amendment will also remove
          the reference to that lot.

(N)       Pursuant to Florida Statutes section 166.031, the Charter may be amended pursuant to a
          referendum put to a vote of the electors at a general election held within the municipality,
          and a referendum to amend the Charter as described in this section will be held in conjunc-
          tion with the municipal general election scheduled for November 3, 2026.

(O)       Because the proposed Charter amendment concerns the scope of property subject to the
          referendum procedures in Charter subsection 1.02(d), the City has elected to follow the
          notice requirements set forth in that subsection and in City Code subsection 30-1(b) with
          respect to the City Infill Lots, and the City Clerk has received proof of compliance with
          those notice requirements.

(P)       Because Florida Statutes subsection 163.3167(8) prohibits the use of a referendum to ap-
          prove certain types of land use regulations, it is prudent to conduct the Charter amendment



00898861 / v03                                   4 of 7                           Jamestown / City Infill Lots
          independently of and prior to beginning any process to modify zoning and future land use
          map designations as described above. Accordingly, the ballot text will clarify that any such
          land use changes would be subsequent to and separate from the referendum called to amend
          the Charter.

(Q)       For the reasons described in these findings, the actions taken by this ordinance serve a valid
          municipal purpose and are in the best interests of the City, and this ordinance is adopted in
          consideration of these findings.

SECTION 2—DATE OF REFERENDUM: The City Council hereby calls the Charter
amendment referendum described in this ordinance (the “Referendum”) to be placed on the ballot
of the municipal general election scheduled for November 3, 2026.

SECTION 3—FORMATTING CONVENTIONS: With respect to the potential amendment
of the Charter set forth in this ordinance, additions are indicated by underlining, deletions are in-
dicated by strikethrough text, and unchanged paragraphs omitted for brevity are indicated by three
ellipsis dots on a separate line.

SECTION 4—CHARTER AMENDMENT: If the Referendum is approved by a majority
vote, the City shall file a revised version of the St. Petersburg City Charter with the Florida De-
partment of State that will amend Charter subsection 1.02 as follows, effective upon filing:

          Sec. 1.02. Park and waterfront property; use, disposition.

                                                .     .      .

          (b)    Definitions. The following terms shall have the definitions listed below
                 when used in this Section 1.02 unless a different meaning is clearly ev-
                 ident from the context in which they appear:

                 (1)     City Park and Waterfront Map. The “City Charter Park and Water-
                         front Map” means the map on file with the City Clerk that was
                         approved in 1984 by the referendum called by Ordinance
                         No. 780-F is and specifically adopted and incorporated herein by
                         reference. (including the original “legal footnotes” that were in-
                         corporated into the map by reference through parenthetical
                         notations on the map key for selected parcels), as subsequently
                         modified by the following textual definitions, which supersede
                         the visual definition on the original map (and the text of the orig-

J-6




      J-6
                                       ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL

                                             Meeting of July 23, 2026


TO:                The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair and Members of City Council

SUBJECT:     Ordinance 640-H, an ordinance concerning the City’s lease of submerged
lands at Harborage Marina; making findings concerning amendment of that lease to
extend the term to facilitate the construction of certain improvements; calling a
referendum during the November 3, 2026 municipal general election to authorize such
amendment; establishing conditions for such amendment if approved by that
referendum; providing ballot text for that referendum; and providing an effective date.

BACKGROUND:         In 1986, a City-wide referendum was held in accordance with
Ordinance 923-F to amend Charter Section 1.02 to authorize the City to enter into a 25-
year submerged land lease for ±11.4 acres of undeveloped submerged land in Bayboro
Harbor. Following execution of such a lease by the City (“1986 Lease”), the tenant
constructed a 255-slip marina facility on and around the leased premises, and this facility
is currently known as Harborage Marina (“Marina”).

In 2004, a second City-wide referendum concerning the Marina was held in accordance
with Charter Section 1.02 that authorized the City to enter into a new, 30-year lease for
the Marina pursuant to conditions stated in Ordinance 673-G (“2004 Referendum”).

In 2007, pursuant to the authorization provided by the 2004 Referendum, the City
entered into a new lease for the Marina that replaced the 1986 Lease. That new lease, as
amended from time-to-time, (“Current Lease”) expanded the premises to a total of
±13.424 acres of submerged land and obligated the tenant under the Current Lease,
Harborage Marina, L.L.C., (“Harborage”), to construct new “mega-yacht” slips at the
Marina. The Current Lease was subsequently amended four (4) times in accordance with
the 2004 Referendum.

In 2018, a third City-wide referendum concerning the Marina was held in accordance
with Charter Section 1.02 and authorized the City to amend the Current Lease for the
Marina pursuant to conditions stated in Ordinance 336-H (“2018 Referendum”). Those
conditions authorized amendment of the Current Lease to (i.) expand the scope of the
leased premises to ±15.33 of submerged land to allow for a new dock and other
improvements, and (ii.) extend the expiration date of the Current Lease to provide 30
years to amortize the cost of those improvements.

On December 21, 2018, a fifth amendment of the Current Lease was executed in
accordance with the 2018 Referendum, and Harborage has performed the obligations
added to the Current Lease by that fifth amendment.

Harborage has requested that the Current Lease be amended again to extend the term
to a full 30 years from the date of amendment to facilitate amortization of approximately
$2.15 million in new improvements to the Marina. The new improvements to be
constructed at no expense to the City would include as follows:



CM 250723 –4 RE Ordinance (Public Hearing) Harborage Marina                               1
         •      Extension of existing docks on the north side of the Marina to
                include a total of 200 linear feet of new docks.
         •      New boat lifts on the east side of the Marina.
         •      Expanded restrooms.

These improvements would become property of the City upon expiration or earlier
termination of the Current Lease, and Harborage has estimated that construction of
those improvements would increase the rent paid to the City by a cumulative amount of
$4,514,140 over a 30-year period. Construction of those improvements would not
require any change to the scope of the premises subject to the Current Lease.

Under City Charter section 1.02, a lease of commercially-zoned waterfront property that
exceeds five years must be approved through a process established by that section,
which requires approval by a majority vote in a City-wide referendum.

Because an amendment to provide the 30-year term requested by Harborage falls within
the scope of that provision, Harborage has requested that the City call a referendum to
approve such an amendment as part of the upcoming municipal general election
scheduled for November 3, 2026.

City Council’s adoption of the proposed ordinance would call such a referendum and
establish conditions for approving the requested amendment if that referendum is
approved.

The City has followed the required procedures for notice of the first reading of this
ordinance under Charter subsection 1.02(d) including City Code subsection 30-1(b). The
City Clerk has received proof of compliance with these notice requirements.

RECOMMENDATION:             Administration recommends that City Council adopt the
attached Ordinance 640-H concerning the City’s lease of submerged lands at Harborage
Marina; making findings concerning amendment of that lease to extend the term to
facilitate the construction of certain improvements; calling a referendum during the
November 3, 2026 municipal general election to authorize such amendment;
establishing conditions for such amendment if approved by that referendum;
providing ballot text for that referendum; and providing an effective date.

ATTACHMENTS:                 Illustration, Ordinance and Presentation

APPROVALS:                   Administration:

                                       Budget:                 N/A

          THE CITY OF ST. PETERSBURG ORDAINS THE FOLLOWING:

SECTION 1—FINDINGS: The City Council of the City of St. Petersburg, Florida, (“City
Council”) hereby makes the following findings:

(A)       In 1986, a City-wide referendum was held in accordance with ordinance 923-F to amend
          Charter section 1.02 to authorize the City to enter into a 25-year submerged land lease for
          ±11.4 acres of undeveloped submerged land in Bayboro Harbor. Following execution of
          such a lease by the City (the “1986 Lease”), the tenant constructed a 255-slip marina facil-
          ity on and around the leased premises, and this facility is currently known as Harborage
          Marina (the “Marina”).

(B)       In 2004, a second City-wide referendum concerning the Marina was held in accordance
          with Charter section 1.02 and authorized the City to enter into a new, 30-year lease for the
          Marina pursuant to conditions stated in ordinance 673-G (the “2004 Referendum”).

(C)       In 2007, pursuant to the authorization provided by the 2004 Referendum, the City entered
          into a new lease for the Marina that replaced the 1986 Lease. That new lease (as amended
          from time-to-time, the “Current Lease”) expanded the premises to a total of ±13.424 acres



00899049 / v06                                   1 of 5                                Harborage Extension
          of submerged land and obligated the tenant under the Current Lease, Harborage Marina,
          L.L.C., (“Harborage”) to construct new “mega-yacht” slips at the Marina.

(D)       The Current Lease was amended four times in accordance with the 2004 Referendum.

(E)       In 2018, a third City-wide referendum concerning the Marina was held in accordance with
          Charter section 1.02 and authorized the City to amend lease for the Marina pursuant to
          conditions stated in ordinance 336-H (the “2018 Referendum”). Those conditions author-
          ized amendment of the Current Lease to (i) expand the scope of the leased premises to
          ±15.33 of submerged land to allow for a new dock and other improvements, and (ii) extend
          the expiration date of the Current Lease to provide 30 years to amortize the cost of those
          improvements.

(F)       On December 21, 2018, a fifth amendment of the Current Lease was executed in accord-
          ance with the 2018 Referendum, and Harborage has performed the obligations added to the
          Current Lease by that fifth amendment.

(G)       Harborage has requested that the Current Lease be amended again to extend the term to a
          full 30 years from the date of amendment to facilitate amortization of approximately $2.15
          million in new improvements to the Marina. Those improvements would be constructed
          at no expense to the City and include:

          (1)    200 linear feet of new docks at the location shown on the diagram attached to this
                 ordinance as exhibit A (the “Improvement Diagram”), along with related fire sup-
                 pression, water, and electric service.

          (2)    New boat lifts at the location shown on the Improvement Diagram.

          (3)    Expanded restrooms.

(H)       Construction of those improvements would not require any change to the scope of the
          premises subject to the Current Lease, and Harborage has estimated that construction of
          those improvements would increase the rent paid to the City by a cumulative amount of
          $4,514,140 over a 30-year period. Additionally, those improvements would become prop-
          erty of the City upon expiration or earlier termination of the Current Lease.

(I)       Under City Charter section 1.02, a lease of commercially-zoned waterfront property that
          exceeds five years must be approved through a process established by that section, which
          requires approval by a majority vote in a City-wide referendum.

(J)       Because an amendment to provide the 30-year term requested by Harborage falls within
          the scope of that provision, Harborage has requested that the City call a referendum to

00899049 / v06                                  2 of 5                               Harborage Extension
          approve such an amendment as part of the upcoming municipal general election scheduled
          for November 3, 2026.

(K)       City Council is adopting this ordinance to call such a referendum and to establish condi-
          tions for approving the requested amendment of the Current Lease if that referendum is
          approved by the voters.

(L)       The City has followed the required procedures for notice of the first reading of this ordi-
          nance under Charter subsection 1.02(d) and City Code subsection 30-1(b), and the City
          Clerk has received proof of compliance with those notice requirements.

(M)       For the reasons described in these findings, the actions taken by this ordinance serve a valid
          municipal purpose and are in the best interests of the City, and this ordinance is adopted in
          consideration of these findings.

SECTION 2—DATE OF REFERENDUM: The City Council hereby calls the referendum
approving the disposition of waterfront property under Charter section 1.02 described in this or-
dinance (the “2026 Referendum”), to be placed on the ballot of the municipal general election
scheduled for November 3, 2026.

SECTION 3—AUTHORIZATION FOR AMENDMENT: If the 2026 Referendum is ap-
proved by a majority vote, the City is authorized to amend the Current Lease subject to the
following conditions (each, an “Amendment”):

(A)       Authority to Amend. Authorization to amend the Current Lease in accordance with this
          section is not limited to a single Amendment, and multiple Amendments may be approved
          pursuant to this section as long as no aspect of an Amendment is inconsistent with the 2004
          Referendum, the 2018 Referendum, or the 2026 Referendum (with any conflict among
          those referenda resolved in favor of the later referendum). But if the first Amendment ap-
          proved in accordance with this section (the “Initial Amendment”) is not fully executed on
          or before December 31, 2027, the authority provided by the 2026 Referendum will expire
          at the end of that date.

(B)       Approval Process. A City Council resolution receiving at least six affirmative votes is
          required to approve each Amendment,

(C)       Premises. The area subject to the Current Lease must not be expanded beyond what was
          authorized under the 2018 Referendum.

(D)       Duration. The maximum duration of the Current Lease must be no more than 30 years
          from the effective date of the Initial Amendment.


00899049 / v06                                    3 of 5                                 Harborage Extension
(E)       Improvements. The Current Lease must require the tenant to complete construction of the
          following improvements at no cost to the City:

          (1)    200 linear feet of new docks at the location shown on the Improvement Diagram,
                 with fire suppression, water, and electric service.

          (2)    New boat lifts at the location shown in the Improvement Diagram.

          (3)    Expanded restrooms.

(F)       Ownership. Pursuant to the terms of the Current Lease, each improvement described in
          subsection (E) becomes property of the City upon expiration or earlier termination of the
          Current Lease.

(G)       Nature of Conditions. These conditions are intended to establish certain minimum stand-
          ards for each Amendment, and they do not prevent an Amendment or the Current Lease
          from including provisions that provide more detail than these conditions or that concern
          matters not mentioned in these conditions.

SECTION 4—BALLOT TEXT: The City shall use the following caption and explanatory
statement as the ballot title and ballot summary for the 2026 Referendum, with the actual ordinance
number inserted prior to submission for use on the applicable ballot:

                 Authorizing Amendment of Harborage Marina Lease to Extend
                 Term to 30 Years and Require Improvements

                 May City Council approve amendment of the lease of City-owned
                 submerged lands at Harborage Marina subject to conditions in
                 ordinance _____? Such amendment would extend the term of
                 the lease to 30 years and require the tenant to construct new
                 improvements at no cost to the City, including 200 linear feet of
                 docks, boat lifts, and expanded restrooms. Those
                 improvements would be owned by the City at the end of the
                 lease.

                 YES
                 NO

SECTION 5—CONFLICT: If any provision of this ordinance conflicts with ordinances 673-G
or 336-H, the provision of this ordinance will control. Otherwise, ordinances 673-G and 336-H
are intended to be unaffected by this ordinance.

The following page(s) contain the backup material for Agenda Item: Ordinance 641-H, An ordinance
concerning lease limitations established for the Port of St. Petersburg under the City Charter; making
findings regarding the need to amend the Charter to increase that limitation; calling a referendum
during the November 3, 2026 municipal general election to authorize such a Charter amendment;
providing ballot text for that referendum; and providing an effective date.
Please scroll down to view the backup material.




                                                 182
J-7




      J-7
                                         ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL

                                               Meeting of July 23, 2026


TO:                 The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair and Members of City Council

SUBJECT:       Ordinance 641-H, an ordinance concerning lease limitations established
for the Port of St. Petersburg under the City Charter; making findings regarding the need
to amend the Charter to increase that limitation; calling a referendum during the
November 3, 2026 municipal general election to authorize such a Charter amendment;
providing ballot text for that referendum; and providing an effective date.

BACKGROUND:        On November 6, 1984, in a City-wide referendum held pursuant to
ordinance 780-F, voters approved an amendment to City Charter section 1.02 for the
purpose of providing certain protections for City-owned park and waterfront property.

Such amendment incorporated into the Charter a map of City-owned park and waterfront
property (“Map”) for the purpose of conclusively determining which parcels of property
owned by the City at that time were considered park or waterfront property, subject to
limitations on leasing, which is defined by the Charter to include “any non-permanent
disposition, of an interest in real property, including but not limited to, leases, permits,
licenses and temporary easements.”

The Port of St. Petersburg is a multi-use, City-owned facility that is located south of the
core downtown area, adjacent to Albert Whitted Airport, on property identified on the
Map as Parcel E (“Port”). The Port features a 1,200-linear-foot wharf and ±4.4 acres of
adjacent uplands that are surrounded by a variety of government, institutional, and
academic organizations engaged in maritime research, with the Port more particularly
described as follows:

          Lot 1, Block 1, PORT OF ST. PETERSBURG REPLAT
          PIN: 30-31-17-72688-001-0010

Current utilization of Port property can be summarized as follows: 1.82% for
administration and support services; 35.68% for cruise operations; and 62.5% for three
principal private tenants—the Cross Bay Ferry, the University of South Florida, and the
St. Pete Innovation District (“SPID”).

SPID utilizes the Port to operate a Maritime and Defense Technology Hub (“Hub”) in a
32,000-square-foot, City-owned building pursuant to a five-year license with the City that
currently runs through March 31, 2029.

The City is currently working to develop a new Master Plan and Reinvestment Strategy
for the Port that would govern the evolution of Port property to meet new economic and
social opportunities over the next 10 years and beyond. The plan is likely to call for the
construction of various capital projects to support and service vessels using the Port
(generally, “Port Redevelopment Projects”). Some of those Port Redevelopment Projects

CM 260723 – 5 RE Ordinance (Public Hearing) Port Lease Limit Increase                      1
are likely to be constructed by private entities relying upon financing that requires
amortization over a multi-year period—frequently up to 25 years.

Additionally, SPID desires to expand the size of the Hub by constructing a new structure
on adjacent property to the west that is currently used for surface parking (“Hub
Expansion”). The Hub Expansion is also likely to rely upon funding sources that require
amortization over a similar multi-year period.

The text of Charter Section 1.02 provides default lease limitations for City-owned park
and waterfront property, with a three (3) year limit for residentially-zoned property and
a five (5) year limit for commercially-zoned property. But the Map establishes longer
lease limitations for 10 of the 109 parcels that are expressly identified on the Map.

The Port is one of those parcels and is designated on the Map as “CITY-OWNED LAND
WITH [A] 10 YEAR LEASE LIMITATION.” Accordingly, any lease of Port property over 10
years can be authorized only through the process established by City Charter Section
1.02, which requires approval by a majority vote in a City-wide referendum.

Because the Hub Expansion and any Port Redevelopment Project constructed by a private
entity are likely to require financing that must be amortized over a period of time
exceeding 10 years, the lease for each such project would need to be approved through
a separate City-wide referendum. With municipal elections held only in even-numbered
years, conducting a separate referendum for each lease will create uncertainty and delay
in the redevelopment process, and it could result in piecemeal redevelopment if only
some of the referendum questions are approved.

Since the Map was incorporated into the Charter in 1984, the Charter has been amended
to increase the duration of some of the original lease limitations, including a 2004
amendment called by Ordinance 677-G that increased the lease limitation for Albert
Whitted Airport from 5 years to 25 years.

Amending the Charter to increase the lease limitation for the Port from 10 years to 25
years would make the Port consistent with Albert Whitted Airport, and it would facilitate
construction of the Hub Expansion and the Port Redevelopment Projects by eliminating
the issues described above. That sort of amendment is contemplated by Charter
subsection 1.02(c)(1), which allows longer limitations added to subsection (c)(4) to
supersede shorter limitations elsewhere in the Charter.

Pursuant to Florida Statutes Section 166.031, the Charter may be amended pursuant to
a referendum put to a vote of the electors at a general election held within the
municipality, and a referendum to amend the Charter as described in this section will be
held in conjunction with the municipal general election scheduled for November 3, 2026.

Because the proposed Charter amendment concerns the scope of property subject to the
referendum procedures in Charter subsection 1.02(d), the City has elected to follow the
notice requirements set forth in that subsection including City Code subsection30-1(b).
The City Clerk has received proof of compliance with these notice requirements.


CM 260723 – 5 RE Ordinance (Public Hearing) Port Lease Limit Increase                   2
RECOMMENDATION:             Administration recommends that City Council adopt the
attached Ordinance 641-H concerning lease limitations established for the Port
of St. Petersburg under the City Charter; making findings regarding the need to
amend the Charter to increase that limitation; calling a referendum during the
November 3, 2026 municipal general election to authorize such a Charter amendment;
providing ballot text for that referendum; and providing an effective date.

                                              195
J-8




      J-8
                                        MEMORANDUM
                      Agenda for the Meetings of July 9 and July 23, 2026

           TO:    Lisset Hanewicz, City Council Chair; Members of City Council

      FROM:       Brett B. Pettigrew, Assistant City Attorney

       DATE:      June 26, 2026

SUBJECT:          Proposed cleanup Charter amendment referendum to fully effectuate the
                  2022 referendum rescheduling of municipal elections to even years



                                                 BODY OF MEM O




       On November 8, 2022, 69.97% of voters in a citywide referendum called pursuant to ordi-
nance 510-H approved amendment of the City Charter to reschedule municipal elections from odd-
numbered years to even-numbered years (the “2022 Referendum”). As stated in the ballot text
approved by those voters, the purpose of the 2022 Referendum was to “hold those elections in
conjunction with national and state elections in even-numbered years beginning in 2024.”

        Unfortunately, that ordinance inadvertently omitted some textual amendments to the Char-
ter that were necessary to fully effectuate the voter-approved transition to even-year municipal
elections. To address those omissions for the 2024 and 2026 municipal election cycles, City Coun-
cil adopted ordinances 586-H and 625-H, respectively, to confirm that certain language remaining
in the Charter after the 2022 Referendum had been superseded and to ratify election dates that
conformed to the 2022 Referendum.

       To eliminate the need to adopt such ratification ordinances for future election cycles, the
City Attorney’s Office recommends placing the attached Charter amendment on the ballot of the
2026 municipal general election. Generally speaking, that amendment would accomplish three
main goals for “cleanup” purposes:

•      Conform the scheduling provisions of Charter section 5.05 to the even-year election schedule
       approved by the 2022 Referendum.

•      Make a variety of technical, clarifying, and conforming changes to Charter section 5.05 to
       improve readability and consistency and to reflect current election administration practices.

•      Conform the scheduling provisions of Charter section 8.01 to the even-year election schedule
       approved by the 2022 Referendum by scheduling the decennial Charter Review Commission
       process to begin in years ending in nine and end in years ending in zero (e.g., 2029–2030).



00899434 / v01                                 1 of 2
       Although this proposed amendment has not been discussed at any Council committee, the
cleanup changes that it would effectuate are consistent with the 2022 Referendum, with current
administrative practices, and with common understanding of the Charter's requirements for hold-
ing a municipal election.

        I look forward to discussing the proposed ordinance with you at the City Council meetings
of July 9 and July 23, 2026. In the meantime, please do not hesitate to contact me directly if you
have any questions about the ordinance prior to those meetings.

                                   ORDINANCE NO. _______


                         AN ORDINANCE CONCERNING AMEND-
                         MENT OF THE CITY CHARTER FOR THE
                         PURPOSE OF FULLY EFFECTUATING
                         THE 2022 REFERENDUM THAT RE-
                         SCHEDULED MUNICIPAL ELECTIONS
                         FOR MAYOR AND CITY COUNCIL MEM-
                         BERS TO OCCUR IN CONJUNCTION
                         WITH NATIONAL AND STATE ELEC-
                         TIONS IN EVEN-NUMBERED YEARS;
                         MAKING FINDINGS CONCERNING THE
                         NEED TO MAKE SUCH AN AMENDMENT;
                         CALLING A REFERENDUM DURING THE
                         NOVEMBER 3, 2026 MUNICIPAL GEN-
                         ERAL ELECTION TO AUTHORIZE SUCH
                         AN AMENDMENT; PROVIDING BALLOT
                         TEXT FOR THAT REFERENDUM; AND
                         PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.


         THE CITY OF ST. PETERSBURG ORDAINS THE FOLLOWING:

SECTION 1—FINDINGS: The City Council of the City of St. Petersburg, Florida, (“City
Council”) hereby makes the following findings:

(A)      On November 8, 2022, 69.97% of voters in a citywide referendum called pursuant to ordi-
         nance 510-H approved amendment of the City Charter to reschedule municipal elections
         from odd-numbered years to even-numbered years (the “2022 Referendum”).

(B)      The ballot text for the 2022 Referendum was as follows:

                  Rescheduling municipal elections to occur in conjunction with
                  national and state elections in even years

                  The City Charter currently requires elections for Mayor and Council
                  Members to occur in odd-numbered years. Shall the Charter be
                  amended to hold those elections in conjunction with national and
                  state elections in even-numbered years beginning in 2024? That
                  change will extend the term of current City elected officials by


00899590 / v03S                                 1 of 8                           Election Schedule Cleanup
                  approximately one year and is intended to increase voter turnout,
                  add early voting at no additional cost, and reduce cost to the City.

                  YES
                  NO

(C)      Although Ordinance 510-H contained a textual amendment of Charter section 3.02 that
         provided for a transition from odd-year elections to even-year elections, the ordinance in-
         advertently omitted other textual amendments to the Charter that were necessary to fully
         effectuate the voter-approved transition to even-year municipal elections.

(D)      For the 2024 and 2026 municipal election cycles, City Council adopted ordinances 586-H
         and 625-H, respectively, to confirm that certain language remaining in the Charter after the
         2022 Referendum was superseded and no longer applied and to ratify election dates that
         conformed to the 2022 Referendum. Amending the Charter to remove that superseded
         language will eliminate the need to adopt such ratifying ordinances for future municipal
         election cycles.

(E)      Accordingly, the Charter should be amended to make technical, clarifying, and conforming
         changes that are necessary and appropriate to fully effectuate the 2022 Referendum. Those
         “cleanup” amendments can be generally described as follows:

         (1)      Charter section 5.05 should be amended to require that municipal primary and gen-
                  eral elections will occur on the same dates as the state primary and general
                  elections. That section should also clarify that, if the state returns to a two-primary
                  system (which it had through 2005 and which recent legislation proposed the rein-
                  troduction of), the municipal primary election will coincide with the first state
                  primary election, rather than the second state primary election (which would be a
                  smaller, runoff election). That approach will ensure that municipal elections remain
                  aligned with state elections regardless of any future change to the schedule for state
                  elections.

         (2)      Charter section 5.05 should also be amended to improve readability (the relevant
                  portions are currently large blocks of text that are difficult to parse) and consistency
                  (with state law and with other provisions of the Charter) and to reflect current prac-
                  tices for administration of elections (e.g., that voting takes place in multiple polling
                  places established by the Pinellas County Supervisor of Elections, rather than a
                  single place established by the City).

         (3)      Charter section 8.01 should be amended to require that the Charter Review Com-
                  mission (“CRC”) will be formed in years ending in nine and that any amendment


00899590 / v03S                                   2 of 8                              Election Schedule Cleanup
                  proposed by CRC will be placed on the ballot of municipal general election in years
                  ending in zero. That approach is consistent with the 2022 Referendum, which
                  moved future elections later by one year to accomplish the transition to even-year
                  elections.

(F)      Pursuant to Florida Statutes section 166.031, the Charter may be amended pursuant to a
         referendum put to a vote of the electors at a general election held within the municipality,
         and a referendum to amend the Charter as described in this section will be held in conjunc-
         tion with the municipal general election scheduled for November 3, 2026.

(G)      For the reasons described in these findings, the actions taken by this ordinance serve a valid
         municipal purpose and are in the best interests of the City, and this ordinance is adopted in
         consideration of these findings.

SECTION 2—DATE OF REFERENDUM: The City Council hereby calls the Charter
amendment referendum described in this ordinance (the “Referendum”) to be placed on the ballot
of the municipal general election scheduled for November 3, 2026.

SECTION 3—FORMATTING CONVENTIONS: With respect to the potential amendment
of the Charter set forth in this ordinance, additions are indicated by underlining, deletions are in-
dicated by strikethrough text, and unchanged paragraphs omitted for brevity are indicated by three
ellipsis dots on a separate line.

SECTION 4—CHARTER AMENDMENT: If the Referendum is approved by a majority
vote, the City shall file a revised version of the St. Petersburg City Charter with the Florida De-
partment of State that will amend the Charter as stated in this section, with those amendments
effective upon filing.

(A)      Charter section 5.05, subsections (a)–(b), will be amended as follows:

         Sec. 5.05. Elections.

         (a)      Primary elections.

                  (1)    On the Tuesday which is ten weeks prior to each general munici-
                         pal election, there shall be held date of each state primary
                         election, the City shall hold a nonpartisan primary election for the
                         nomination of candidates for Council Members and Mayor, if ap-
                         plicable, which primary shall be called by the Council by proper
                         resolution,. If there are two state primary elections, the municipal
                         primary election will be held on the date of the earlier of the two
                         state primary elections.


00899590 / v03S                                  3 of 8                             Election Schedule Cleanup
                  (2)   notice of which Notice of the municipal primary election shall be
                        published for five consecutive days in a daily newspaper pub-
                        lished of general circulation in the City, which shall contain a list
                        of such candidates, the offices to be filled and the time when and
                        the place where such election shall be held, and the . That notice
                        must include the date of the election, a list of the offices and
                        candidates that will appear on the ballot for the election, and in-
                        formation about methods of voting in the election (which may be
                        provided through reference to information provided by the Super-
                        visor of Elections). The last publication of that notice shall
                        appear at least ten (10) days prior to the date of holding said the
                        primary election.

                  (3)   The two candidates from each district receiving the highest num-
                        ber of votes cast by the electors of said district shall be declared
                        the primary nominees, and shall be entitled to have their names
                        printed placed on the ballot to be used in the general municipal
                        election.

                  (4)   If a candidate for Mayor receives more than fifty percent (50%)
                        of the votes cast in the primary election, then that candidate shall
                        be considered duly elected and shall not be required to be placed
                        on the general election ballot. If no candidate for Mayor receives
                        more than fifty percent (50%) of the vote in the primary election,
                        then the two candidates for Mayor receiving the highest number
                        of votes cast by the electors at large shall be declared the primary
                        nominees, and shall be entitled to have their names printed on
                        the ballot to be used in the general municipal election.

                  (5)   Only the names entitled to be on the ballot pursuant to this sSub-
                        section 5.05(a) and no others shall be certified by the City Clerk
                        as candidates duly nominated for office.

                  (6)   A tie between two (2) or more candidates for the same nomina-
                        tion shall be decided by lot under the direction of the City Clerk.

                  (7)   If there are only two (2) candidates for nomination for an office
                        in a primary election, no primary election shall be held for that
                        office and both candidates shall be certified by the City Clerk as
                        candidates duly nominated for that office.




00899590 / v03S                                4 of 8                             Election Schedule Cleanup
                  (8)    In situations where If there is only one candidate for nomination
                         to an office, neither the primary election nor the general election
                         will be held for that office and the candidate shall be declared
                         elected to that office.

         (b)      General elections.

                  (1)    On the first Tuesday following the first Monday in November of
                         each odd-numbered year date of each state general election, the
                         City shall hold a municipal general municipal election shall be
                         held.

                  (2)    The City Clerk, on or before the tenth day before the general mu-
                         nicipal election, shall cause a notice of that election to be
                         published calling such election for three (3) consecutive days in
                         a daily newspaper published of general circulation in the City,
                         which shall contain a list of such candidates, the office to be
                         filled, and the time when and the place where such election will
                         be heldmust include the same items required under subsec-
                         tion (a) for notice of a primary election.

                  (3)    In the general municipal election, the electors of the City at large
                         shall, from among the candidates on the ballot, vote for one can-
                         didate from each district and, if applicable, for one candidate for
                         Mayor; the candidate from each district receiving the highest
                         number of votes shall be declared elected as Council Member
                         from said district, and the candidate for Mayor receiving the high-
                         est number of votes shall be declared elected as Mayor.

                  (4)    A tie between two (2) or more candidates for the same office shall
                         be decided by lot under the direction of the City Clerk.

                                               .     .      .

(B)      Charter section 8.01, subsections (a), (b), (d), and (f), will be amended as follows:

         Sec. 8.01. Charter Review Commission.

         (a)      Purpose; Frequency. A Charter Review Commission consisting of nine
                  members must be established every ten years for the purpose of review-
                  ing, on behalf of the citizens of St. Petersburg, the operation of City
                  government and proposing amendments to this Charter to be placed on



00899590 / v03S                                    5 of 8                          Election Schedule Cleanup
                  the ballot of the municipal general election occurring in each year end-
                  ing in nine zero.

         (b)      Appointments.

                  (1)    No later than August 1 of each year ending in eight nine, the
                         Mayor, with approval of City Council, shall establish a standard
                         application to be used by each person applying to serve on the
                         Charter Review Commission. At a minimum, that standard appli-
                         cation must be submitted prior to appointment and must
                         confirm, under penalty of perjury, that the applicant satisfies the
                         qualifications established by this section. This provision does not
                         prevent a Council Member or the Mayor from requesting addi-
                         tional information from an applicant through a supplemental
                         application, interview, or otherwise.

                  (2)    No later than September 15 of each year ending in eight nine,
                         each Council Member and the Mayor shall appoint one member
                         to the Charter Review Commission by submitting the name of
                         that appointment in writing to the City Clerk.

                                               .     .      .

         (d)      Procedures.

                                               .     .      .

                  (2)    In November of each year ending in eight nine, the Charter Re-
                         view Commission shall hold an organizational meeting, and at
                         that organizational meeting, the body shall elect a Chair and Vice
                         Chair from among its membership. Further meetings of the Char-
                         ter Review Commission shall be held upon the call of the Chair
                         or any three members of the Charter Review Commission. A ma-
                         jority of the members of the Charter Review Commission
                         constitutes a quorum.

                                               .     .      .

         (f)      Report and Amendments.

                                               .     .      .




00899590 / v03S                                    6 of 8                        Election Schedule Cleanup
                  (2)    The Charter Review Commission shall submit the report required
                         by this section no later than the earliest to occur of the following
                         dates in each year ending in nine zero: (i) July 31 preceding the
                         applicable election or (ii) 15 days prior to the last regular meeting
                         of City Council scheduled to occur before the deadline estab-
                         lished by the Supervisor of Elections for placing a charter
                         amendment on the ballot of the applicable election. Following
                         submission of the report in accordance with this subsection (2),
                         the Chair of City Council shall place that report on the agenda of
                         a regular meeting of City Council prior to the deadline established
                         by the Supervisor of Elections.

                                               .     .      .

SECTION 5—BALLOT TEXT: The City shall use the following caption and explanatory
statement as the ballot title and ballot summary for the Referendum:

                  Technical, Clarifying, and Conforming Changes to Effectuate
                  Holding Municipal Elections In Even-Numbered Years

                  In 2022, voters in a citywide referendum approved amendment
                  of the City Charter to reschedule elections for Mayor and
                  Council Members to even-numbered years so that City elections
                  would align with national and state elections beginning in 2024.
                  Shall the Charter be amended to make technical, clarifying, and
                  conforming changes necessary and appropriate to fully
                  effectuate the intent of the 2022 referendum, including
                  alignment of the Charter-review process with the current
                  election schedule?

                  YES
                  NO

SECTION 6—SEVERABILITY: The provisions of this ordinance are intended to be severa-
ble, and a determination that any portion of this ordinance is invalid should not affect the validity
of the remaining portions of this ordinance.

SECTION 7—COMPLIANCE WITH § 166.041(4), FLORIDA STATUTES . Pursuant
to City Council resolution 2023-507, a business impact estimate was prepared for this ordinance
and posted on the City’s website no later than the date the notice of the proposed ordinance was
published.



00899590 / v03S                                    7 of 8                          Election Schedule Cleanup
SECTION 8—EFFECTIVE DATE: In the event that this ordinance is not vetoed by the
Mayor in accordance with the City Charter, it shall become effective after the fifth business day
after adoption unless the Mayor notifies the City Council through written notice filed with the City
Clerk that the Mayor will not veto the ordinance, in which case the ordinance shall take effect
immediately upon filing such written notice with the City Clerk. In the event this ordinance is
vetoed by the Mayor in accordance with the City Charter, it shall not become effective unless and
until the City Council overrides the veto in accordance with the City Charter, in which case it shall
become effective immediately upon a successful vote to override the veto.

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Accepting a bid and approving award of an agreement with Hanover Uniform Company
for custom uniform and apparel services for citywide use in the amount of $950,000.

Explanation: The Procurement and Supply Management Department issued IFB- 26-208 on April
15, 2026. The bids were due on May 14, 2026, and opened thereafter. The Procurement and
Supply Management Department received fourteen bids for custom uniform and apparel services
and are tabulated as follows:

   Bidders                                                               Amount
   Chef Works                                                          $89,812.40
   Hanover Uniform Company                                              99,257.75
   Custom Concepts of St. Petersburg, Inc.                             110,130.00
   Young Guns Embroidery, Inc.                                         110,695.80
   Active Promotions                                                   111,718.25
   Quokka Brew                                                         113,456.00
   WB Mason                                                            116,296.25
   Keyport Army Navy                                                   117,165.70
   Global Trading, Inc                                                 125,887.75
   Touch Printing LLC                                                  126,295.75
   Vampt Co                                                            130,265.10
   Suncoast Promotional Products, Inc.                                 137,651.35
   Grand Illusion Creative Resources                                   162,656.50
   Craft-Tee LLC                                                       171,966.00

The Procurement and Supply Management Department recommends for award:

                 Hanover Uniform Company (Baltimore, MD) ……… $950,000
                             (Three-years @ 316,666 per year)

Chef Works withdrew their bid citing an inability to honor their submitted market basket pricing;
accordingly, Hanover Uniform Company, the lowest responsive and responsible bidder, has met
the requirements of IFB-26-208, dated April 15, 2026. An agreement will be issued for the initial
3-year term. The agreement will have 2, 1-year renewal options and will be binding only for actual
products delivered.

Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funds have been previously appropriated in the
General Fund (0001), Various Departments, Various Divisions; Stormwater Utility Fund (4011),
Stormwater, Pavement and Traffic Operations Department (400), Various Divisions; Sanitation
Operating Fund (4021), Sanitation Department (450), Various Divisions; and the Water
Resources Operating Fund (4001), Water Resources Department (420), Various Divisions.

                        A RESOLUTION ACCEPTING THE BID AND
                        APPROVING THE AWARD OF A THREE-YEAR
                        AGREEMENT WITH TWO ONE-YEAR RENEWAL
                        OPTIONS   WITH    HANOVER     UNIFORM
                        COMPANY FOR CUSTOM UNIFORM AND
                        APPAREL SERVICES FOR CITYWIDE USE FOR
                        A TOTAL CONTRACT AMOUNT NOT TO
                        EXCEED $950,000 FOR THE INITIAL TERM;
                        AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR HIS DESIGNEE
                        TO EXECUTE ALL DOCUMENTS NECESSARY
                        TO EFFECTUATE THIS TRANSACTION; AND
                        PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.

     WHEREAS, on April 15, 2026, the Procurement & Supply Management Department issued IFB No.
26-208 for custom uniform and apparel services for the Procurement and Supply Management Department;
and

      WHEREAS, the Procurement & Supply Management Department received fourteen (14) bids in
response to IFB No. 26-208, however, Chef Works withdrew their bid; and

      WHEREAS, Hanover Uniform Company, the lowest responsible and responsive bidder, has met the
 requirements of IFB No. 26-208; and

     WHERAS, the city desires to accept the bid to enter into a three-year Agreement with two one-year
 renewal options with Hanover Uniform Company for a total contract amount not to exceed $950,000; and

       WHEREAS, the Procurement & Supply Management Department recommends approval of this
resolution; and

       NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of the City of St. Petersburg,
Florida, that the bid is accepted and the award of a three-year agreement with two one-year renewal
options with Hanover Uniform Company for custom uniform and apparel services for city wide use for a
total contract amount not to exceed $950,000 for the initial term is hereby approved.

      BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to
execute all documents necessary to effectuate this transaction.

       This resolution shall become effective immediately upon its adoption.

                              ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL
                                      Consent Agenda
                                   Meeting of July 23, 2026


To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving the renewal of an agreement with Palmdale Oil Company, LLC for marine
grade gasoline and diesel fuel in the amount of $3,500,000.

Explanation: The vendor furnishes and delivers unleaded 90 octane gasoline without ethanol,
45 octane ultralow sulfur diesel No.2 off-road red-dyed, and marine fuel additives in tank wagon
loads of 4,400 gallons or less. This fuel is used for resale to the general boating public at the St.
Petersburg Marina and to the Marine Unit of the Police Department. The pricing is based on a
fixed delivery price per gallon with an estimated annual delivery of 350,000 gallons of fuel. The
price per gallon of fuel is based on the Port of Tampa average terminal rack prices published daily
in Oil Price Information Service (OPIS). Fuel pricing is priced the day the order is placed, plus the
fixed fee for delivery and applicable taxes.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Enterprise
Facilities Department, recommends for renewal:

          Palmdale Oil Company, LLC (Palm Beach Gardens, FL) ……… $3,500,000


The original agreement was executed on August 1, 2024 and $2,451,092.24 has been spent to
date. The vendor has agreed to renew under the same terms and conditions. Administration
recommends renewal of the agreement based on vendor’s past satisfactory performance and
demonstrated ability to comply with the terms and conditions of the agreement. The renewal will
be effective from August 1, 2026, through July 31, 2027, with a one-year renewal option
remaining. Payment will be binding only for actual services rendered.


Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funds have been previously appropriated in the
Marina Operating Fund (4041), Enterprise Facilities Department, Marina Division (282-1885); and
the General Fund (0001), Police Department, Traffic and Marine Division (140-1477).

Attachments: Resolution
                                     RESOLUTION NO. 2026-____

                 TERM THROUGH JULY 31, 2027 AND INCREASE THE TOTAL

                 TERM; AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR HIS DESIGNEE TO

                 THIS TRANSACTION; AND PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.

       WHEREAS, on August 1, 2024, the City entered into an agreement with Palmdale Oil Company,
LLC for marine grade gasoline and diesel fuel for the Enterprise Facilities Department (“Agreement”); and

        WHEREAS, the total spend to date for this contract is $2,451,092.24; and

        WHEREAS, the City desires to exercise the renewal option in the Agreement to extend the term
through July 31, 2027, and increase the total contract amount by $3,500,000 for the renewal term; and

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving the renewal of an agreement with The Howard E Nyhart Company
Incorporated for actuarial services for pension programs and other post-employment benefits in
the amount of $609,220.

Explanation: The vendor provides professional actuarial services including valuation, reporting,
consultation and advisory services for the city’s three pension programs and other post-
employment benefits (OPEB). These services include cost studies for proposed pension and
OPEB changes, and annual financial disclosures for the three retirement systems, per the
requirements of Statement 67 and 68 of the Government Accounting Standards Board (GASB
67/68).

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Human
Resources Department, recommends for renewal:

        The Howard E Nyhart Company Incorporated (Indianapolis, IN) ……… $609,220
                     (Three-years @ approximately $203,073.33/year)


The original agreement was executed on January 26, 2022 and $677,829.90 has been spent
during the initial term. The vendor has agreed to renew under the same terms and conditions.
Administration recommends renewal of the agreement based on vendor’s past satisfactory
performance and demonstrated ability to comply with the terms and conditions of the agreement.
The renewal will be effective from November 1, 2026 through October 31, 2029 with no renewals
remaining. Payment will be binding only for actual services rendered.


Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funds have been previously appropriated in the
General Fund (0001), Human Resources Department, Pension Management Division (090.1921)
and the Health Insurance Fund (5121), Human Resources Department, Group Benefits Division
(090.1177). Funding for future years will be included in the City’s annual operating budget subject
to approval by City Council.

saws, screwdrivers, and pliers. They also furnish and deliver hardware items such as flashlights,
abrasives, rope, lubricants, and spray paint. These items are stocked at the Consolidated
Warehouse (CW).

                                                247
CA-5




       CA-5
                              ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL

                                          Consent Agenda

                                      Meeting of July 23, 2026



To:    The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair and Members of City Council
Subject: A resolution authorizing the Mayor or his designee to apply for assistance from the State
of Florida, Department of Environmental Protection under the State Revolving Fund (SRF) loan
program for drinking water facilities for the Washington Terrace Pumping and Storage Project;
identifying pledged revenues for repayment of the loan; authorizing the Mayor or his designee to
execute the loan agreement, subject to the approval of the City Attorney’s Office and Chief
Financial Officer or her designee; providing that the Mayor or his designee will be responsible for
carrying out the City’s responsibilities set forth in the loan agreement and for assuring compliance
with the SRF loan program requirements; and providing an effective date.
Explanation: The American Relief Act (ARA), 2025, P.L. 118-158 requires the State Revolving
Fund (SRF) to administer the Supplemental Appropriations for Hurricanes Helene and Milton and
the Hawai'i Wildfires (SAHM). SRF requirements and procedures apply to these supplemental
funds. The Department has determined that funds in the amount of approximately $844.7 million are
available in State Fiscal Year (SFY) 2026 to the Drinking Water State Revolving Fund (DWSRF).
The ARA SAHM funding will be 50% subsidization (principal forgiveness) with a 50% loan
component for all eligible systems.

The City of St. Petersburg has been selected to proceed with submitting a Drinking Water State
Revolving Fund Loan Application for Oberly Vertical Turbine Project. The loan program will
provide subsidized funding to complete much needed improvements to this drinking water facility.
On March 23rd, 2026 City Council originally approved resolution 2026-133 for the application to
Oberley Vertical Turbine project; however the Water Resources Department in collaboration with
the Engineering Department recommends including the Washington Terrace Pumping and Storage
Project to the application in the case that there are opportunities for future funding allocations, and
to simplify the procurement process for both facilities.
Recommendation: Based on the above information, administration recommends City Council
approve the attached resolution authorizing the Mayor or his designee to apply for assistance from
the State of Florida, Department of Environmental Protection under the State Revolving Fund
(SRF) loan program for drinking water facilities for the Washington Terrace Pumping and Storage
Project; identifying pledged revenues for repayment of the loan; authorizing the Mayor or his
designee to execute the loan agreement, subject to the approval of the City Attorney’s Office and
Chief Financial Officer or her designee; providing that the Mayor or his designee will be
responsible for carrying out the city’s responsibilities set forth in the loan agreement and for
assuring compliance with the SRF loan program requirements; and providing an effective date



                                                  1
Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: The City of St. Petersburg would agree to a loan
structure in which, if awarded, of the $35,139,169 awarded to the City, 50% of the total amount
would be repaid in a long-term loan with 0% interest. The total cost to the City for this project
would equal $17,569,584.

Attachments:
Resolution
Department of Environmental Protection Drinking Water Supplemental Appropriation for
Hurricanes Helene and Milton and Hawaii Wildfires Capitalization Grant Priority List

                                RESOLUTION NO. 2026 - ____

                      A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE
                      MAYOR OR HIS DESIGNEE TO APPLY FOR
                      ASSISTANCE FROM THE STATE OF
                      FLORIDA,       DEPARTMENT        OF
                      ENVIRONMENTAL PROTECTION UNDER
                      THE STATE REVOLVING FUND (“SRF”)
                      LOAN PROGRAM FOR DRINKING WATER
                      FACILITIES FOR THE WASHINGTON
                      TERRACE PUMPING AND STORAGE
                      PROJECT;    IDENTIFYING     PLEDGED
                      REVENUES FOR REPAYMENT OF THE
                      LOAN; AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR
                      HIS DESIGNEE TO EXECUTE THE LOAN
                      AGREEMENT,     SUBJECT     TO   THE
                      APPROVAL OF THE CITY ATTORNEY’S
                      OFFICE AND CHIEF FINANCIAL OFFICER
                      OR HER DESIGNEE; PROVIDING THAT
                      THE MAYOR OR HIS DESIGNEE WILL BE
                      RESPONSIBLE FOR CARRYING OUT THE
                      CITY’S RESPONSIBILITIES SET FORTH IN
                      THE LOAN AGREEMENT AND FOR
                      ASSURING COMPLIANCE WITH THE SRF
                      LOAN PROGRAM REQUIREMENTS; AND
                      PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.

       WHEREAS, the City Council of the City of St. Petersburg (the “City Council”) has
determined it to be in the best interest of the City of St. Petersburg (the “City”) to apply for a
loan from the State of Florida, Department of Environmental Protection under the SRF Loan
Program for Drinking Water Facilities for the Washington Terrace Pumping and Storage Project.

        NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of St. Petersburg, Florida
that:

       Section 1.      The Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to submit a loan
application to the State of Florida, Department of Environmental Protection under the SRF Loan
Program for Drinking Water Facilities for the Washington Terrace Pumping and Storage Project.

       Section 2.     The loan will be repaid from Net Revenues of the System on a
subordinated basis to all Bonds (as such terms are defined in the hereinafter defined Bond
Resolution) issued pursuant to Resolution No. 99-227 adopted by the City Council on April 22,
1999, as amended and supplemented from time to time, as particularly amended by Resolution



                                            Page 1 of 2
No. 2005-559 adopted by the City Council on October 20, 2005, Resolution No. 2008-256
adopted by the City Council on May 15, 2008, and Resolution No. 2013-400 adopted by the City
Council on October 3, 2013 (collectively, the "Bond Resolution").

       Section 3. The Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to execute the loan agreement,
subject to the approval of the City Attorney’s Office and the Chief Financial Officer or her
designee.

       Section 4.      The Mayor or his designee is responsible for carrying out the City’s
responsibilities set forth in the loan agreement and assuring compliance with the SRF Loan
Program requirements.

       This Resolution shall take effect immediately upon adoption.

                                               265
                             ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL
                                     Consent Agenda
                                  Meeting of July 23, 2026


To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving an agreement with Core & Main LP, a sole source supplier, for Sensus
residential and commercial water meters, for the Water Resources Department, in the amount of
$10,000,000.

Explanation: The vendor will furnish and deliver water meters for potable and reclaimed water
for both residential and commercial use. The water meters will be stocked at the Consolidated
Warehouse. Core & Main LP is the only authorized distributor of Sensus water meters and
products in the State of Florida.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Water Resources
Department, recommends for approval:

               Core & Main LP (St. Louis, MO) …………………….. $10,000,000

This purchase is being made in accordance with Section 2-196 (a)(5) of the Procurement Code,
which allows for a sole source procurement to be used for the purchases of supplies, services, or
construction when such supply, services, or construction is available from only one source. The
Procurement and Supply Management Director has reviewed the sole source documents and
determined the purchase of Sensus residential and commercial water meters shall be made a
sole source. An agreement will be issued for the initial five-year term. The agreement has
successive on-going renewal options based on mutual consent of both parties and will be binding
only for actual products delivered and services rendered.

Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funds have been previously appropriated in the
Water Resources Capital Projects Fund (4003) DIS Backflow Meter Replacement FY26 Project
(21414). Funding for future years will be included in the City’s annual budget and is subject to
approval by City Council.

Attachments: Resolution
                                  RESOLUTION NO. 2026-_____

                       A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE AWARD
                       OF A     FIVE-YEAR AGREEMENT WITH
                       SUCCESSIVE ON-GOING RENEWAL OPTIONS
                       WITH CORE & MAIN LP FOR SENSUS
                       RESIDENTIAL AND COMMERCIAL WATER
                       METERS FOR THE WATER RESOURCES
                       DEPARTMENT FOR A TOTAL CONTRACT
                       AMOUNT NOT TO EXCEED $10,000,000 FOR
                       THE INITIAL TERM; AUTHORIZING THE
                       MAYOR OR HIS DESIGNEE TO EXECUTE ALL
                       DOCUMENTS NECESSARY TO EFFECTUATE
                       THIS TRANSACTION; AND PROVIDING AN
                       EFFECTIVE DATE.

       WHEREAS, the City desires to enter into a five-year agreement with successive on-going
renewal options with Core & Main LP for Sensus residential and commercial water meters for the Water
Resources Department for a total contract amount not to exceed $10,000,000 for the initial term; and

        WHEREAS, this purchase is being made in accordance with Section 2-196 (a) of the
Procurement Code, which allows for a sole source procurement to be used for the purchases of supplies,
services, or construction when such supply, services, or construction is available from only one source;
and

         WHEREAS, the Procurement and Supply Management Director has reviewed the sole source
documents and determined the purchase of Sensus residential and commercial water meters shall be made
as a sole source procurement; and

       WHEREAS, the Procurement & Supply Management Department in cooperation with the Water
Resources Department recommends approval of this Resolution; and

         NOW, THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of the City of St. Petersburg,
Florida, that the award of a five-year agreement with successive on-going renewal options with Core &
Main LP for Sensus residential and commercial water meters for the Water Resources Department for a
total contract amount not to exceed $10,000,000 for the initial term is hereby approved.

        BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to
execute all documents necessary to effectuate this transaction.

       This resolution shall become effective immediately upon its adoption.
