CB-1




       CB-1
                              ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL
                                      Consent Agenda
                                   Meeting of July 23, 2026


To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council
Subject: Approving the donation of three Ford Interceptors that have been fully depreciated to
the Florida A&M University Police Department.
Explanation: It is recommended that three Ford Interceptors be donated to Florida A&M
University Police Department for their use. The assets are fully depreciated and there is no cost
impact to the city. The vehicles are customized for only police services and cannot be re-purposed
for use in other city departments.
The three Ford Interceptors were acquired in October 2015 for one vehicle at $28,145 and
December 2015 for two vehicles at $29,844. The vehicles have completed their life in service of
7 years for the City of St Petersburg. The assets are in fair condition.
This donation is in accordance with the Administrative Policy 050700 Disposition of Surplus
Property. This policy covers the classification, transfer, and disposal of surplus property. It is the
objective of this program to maximize returns on excess and surplus property by transferring the
property or disposing of it through a public auction, sealed bids, posted prices, trade-in,
destruction or abandonment, or donation.

The Police Department will coordinate the donation of the asset to Florida A&M University Police
Department.
Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Donation of the three Ford Interceptors will have no
impact on the City as the net book value of the asset is zero ($0).
Attachments: Resolution
                                 RESOLUTION NO. 2026-____

                A RESOLUTION APPROVING THE DONATION OF THREE
                FORD INTERCEPTORS THAT HAVE BEEN FULLY
                DEPRECIATED TO FLORIDA A&M UNIVERSITY POLICE
                DEPARTMENT; AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR HIS
                DESIGNEE TO EXECUTE ALL DOCUMENTS NECESSARY
                TO EFFECTUATE THE DONATION OF THESE VEHICLES
                TO FLORIDA A&M UNIVERSITY POLICE DEPARTMENT;
                AND PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.

       WHEREAS, the St. Petersburg Police Department no longer has an operational need for
three Ford Interceptors and Administration desires to donate these vehicles to Florida A&M
University Police Department; and

       WHEREAS, the Fleet Department will transfer the title and all liability and maintenance
responsibility of these vehicles to Florida A&M University Police Department.

        NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of the City of St.
Petersburg, Florida, that the donation of three Ford Interceptors that have been fully depreciated
to Florida A&M University Police Department is hereby approved.

       BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to
execute all documents necessary to effectuate the donation of these vehicles to Florida A&M
University Police Department.

The following page(s) contain the backup material for Agenda Item: Approving the renewal of an
agreement with Clonetab Inc for database cloning, backup, and recovery automation services in the

                                               270
CB-2




       CB-2
                             ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL
                                     Consent Agenda
                                  Meeting of July 23, 2026


To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving the renewal of an agreement with Clonetab Inc for database cloning, backup,
and recovery automation services in the amount of $122,299.62.

Explanation: The vendor will supply the Clonetab CTClone and CTSnap software that includes
an end user License for one instance of Clonetab CTClone cloning solution for eBusiness Suite
12.2.7 with backend Oracle Database 19C on-premises along with CTSnap license for taking
snapshots of six target instances.

This product has aided in the cloning process for EBS non-production instances. A clone usually
takes about 40 hours to complete, this product has shortened it to 8 hours and automated several
tasks to produce stable and consistent clones. A minimum of two clones are created every month.
This has allowed the EBS team to resume tasks quickly with minimal interruption while the
database administrators can focus on upgrades and optimization tasks.


The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Department of
Technology Services, recommends for renewal:

                         Clonetab Inc (Tracy, CA) ……… $122,299.62
                              (Three-years @ $40,766.54/year)


The original agreement was executed on October 17, 2023 and $171,868.80 has been spent to
date. The vendor has agreed to renew under the same terms and conditions. Administration
recommends renewal of the agreement based on vendor’s past satisfactory performance and
demonstrated ability to comply with the terms and conditions of the agreement. The renewal will
be effective from October 17, 2026 through October 16, 2029, with successive renewal options
available. Payment will be binding only for actual services rendered.


Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funding will be available in the Technology Services
Fund (5011), Department of Technology Services, Computer Operations Division (850-2561)
after City Council approval of the FY27 budget. Funding for future years will be included in the
City’s annual operating budget subject to approval by City Council.

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving an agreement with The Computer Solution Company of Virginia, LLC, a sole
source supplier, for a Laboratory Information Management System (LIMS), for the Police
Department, in the amount of $170,300.

Explanation: The vendor will provide, implement, host, and support a Laboratory Information
Management System (LIMS) software for six users. The system will be used to manage forensic
laboratory cases, track evidence through its lifecycle, maintain chain-of-custody records, and
support accreditation and regulatory compliance requirements.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Police
Department, recommends for award:

    The Computer Solution Company of Virginia, LLC (Niagara Falls, NY) ……… $170,300


This purchase is being made in accordance with Section 2-196(a)(5) of the Procurement Code,
which allows for a sole source procurement to be used for the purchase of a supply, service, or
software that is a component or replacement part, an expansion or upgrade, or a specialized
service for which there is no commercially available substitute and which can be purchased only
from the manufacturer, distributor, or a manufacturer's service representative. The Procurement
and Supply Management Director has reviewed all documentation and determined the purchase
of the LIMS software implementation, hosting, and support services shall be made as sole source.
An agreement will be issued for the initial one-year term. The agreement will have subsequent
renewal options and will be binding only for actual services rendered.


Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funding has been previously appropriated in the
General Fund (0001), Police Department, Fiscal Support Division (140-1389), Crime Gun
Intelligence Center Project (19951) and the Local Law Enforcement State Trust Fund (1601),
Police Department, Local Law Enforcement State Trust Division (140-2857).

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving the renewal of an agreement with TriNova, Inc., a sole source supplier, for
chlorination equipment for the Water Resources Department, in the amount of $300,000.

Explanation: The vendor furnishes and delivers equipment used at the wastewater treatment
plants to measure and maintain the proper levels of chlorination in the potable waste
disinfection process. The equipment includes systems that control feed rates, analyze chorine,
bleach, and pH data to provide continuous water monitoring and alarm notification. The City
has standardized the system’s critical OEM replacement parts to De/Nora/Capital Controls, and
ProMinent Fluid Controls to maintain system-wide compatibility and reliability. ProMinent Fluid
Controls and De Nora/Capital Controls equipment are available locally only through TriNova,
Inc., therefore, a sole source procurement is recommended.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Water
Resources Department, recommends for renewal:

                 TriNova, Inc. (Lakeland, FL) ………………………. $300,000

The original agreement was executed on August 3, 2023, and $295,780 has been spent during
the initial term. The vendor has agreed to renew under the same terms and conditions.
Administration recommends renewal of the agreement based on the vendor’s past satisfactory
performance and demonstrated ability to comply with the terms and conditions of the
agreement. The renewal will be effective from August 3, 2026 through August 2, 2028, with no
renewal option remaining. Payment will be made only for goods delivered.

Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funds have been previously appropriated in the
Water Resources Operating Fund (4001), Water Resources Department, Various Divisions.

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving the renewal of an agreement with PFM Financial Advisors LLC for financial
advisory services, as a registered municipal advisor, in the amount of $150,000.

Explanation: The vendor provides debt and investment advising services and will assist with both
negotiated and competitive multiple debt issues (bonds, notes, loans, capital leases and other
debt obligations) whether taxable or tax-exempt, fixed rate or variable. Services may also include
consultation on escrow investment restructuring, arbitrage discussions, disclosure issues,
recruitment and evaluation of outside investment bankers, consulting with bond rating agencies,
coordinating and supervising debt closing, analyzing the bond market and financing opportunities.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department, in cooperation with the Finance
Department, recommends for renewal:

                PFM Financial Advisors LLC (Philadelphia, PA) ……… $150,000


The original agreement was executed on October 1, 2022, and $299,907 has been spent to date.
The vendor has agreed to renew under the same terms and conditions. Administration
recommends renewal of the agreement based on vendor’s past satisfactory performance and
demonstrated ability to comply with the terms and conditions of the agreement. The renewal will
be effective from October 1, 2026 through September 30, 2027, with no renewal options
remaining. Payment will be binding only for actual services rendered.


Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Financial advisory transactional fees are based on
percentage of debt issued and are appropriated from debt proceeds at the time of the issuance
of bonds. Non-transactional fees are based on agreed upon hourly rates and vary based on the
non-transactional work requested by the City and are paid from the appropriate annual operating
budget.

 To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Accepting a bid and approving award of an agreement with Prison Rehabilitative
Industries and Diversified Enterprises, Inc. for Office Paper Products for the Procurement and
Supply Management Department, in the amount of $420,000.

Explanation: The Procurement and Supply Management Department issued two Invitations for
Bids (IFBs); however, both solicitations were unsuccessful because all bids received were
deemed non-responsive, resulting in the disqualification of all vendors and the closure of both
solicitations without an award.

To ensure the City's operational needs were met in the most efficient and cost-effective manner,
The Procurement and Supply Management Department Director determined that direct
negotiations with prospective vendors were in the City's best interest pursuant to Section 2-191(k)
of the City's Procurement Code.

Negotiations were conducted with Mac Papers & Packaging, W.B. Mason, and Prison
Rehabilitative Industries and Diversified Enterprises, Inc. The estimated annual pricing submitted
by each participating vendor is as follows:

                                                                             Yearly Amount
 Prison Rehabilitative Industries and Diversified Enterprises, Inc (Brandon, $134,857.45
 FL)
 W.B. Mason (Brockton, MA)                                                   166,920.30
 Mac Papers & Packaging (Tampa, FL)                                          218,162.10


The vendor will furnish and deliver office paper products, including, but not limited to, customized
utility billing envelopes, utility billing paper, and copier paper. Products will be delivered to the City
of St. Petersburg’s Consolidated Warehouse on an as-needed basis.

        The Procurement and Supply Management Department recommends for award:

        Prison Rehabilitative Industries and Diversified Enterprises, Inc.............$420,000
                               (Three-years @ $140,000 per year)

Prison Rehabilitative Industries and Diversified Enterprises, Inc. has been determined to be the
lowest responsive and responsible bidder. An agreement will be issued for the initial three-year
term. The agreement will have two, one-year renewal options and will be binding only for actual
products delivered.

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: Approving an agreement with MentorAPM, LLC, a sole source supplier, for MentorLENS
software, implementation, and maintenance, for the Asset Management Office, in the amount
of $400,000.

Explanation: The vendor will furnish MentorLENS, a cloud-based software subscription that
converts field photographs of equipment nameplates into structured asset data and uses AI to
assess equipment condition from photos and thermal imaging. The software integrates directly
with the City's Oracle Work & Asset Cloud Service (WACS) to load asset data and condition
assessments into the City's asset register. MentorAPM is the only vendor offering this combined
nameplate-to-data and AI condition assessment workflow with native Oracle WACS integration.

The Procurement and Supply Management Department,           in   cooperation   with   the   Asset
Management Office, recommends for approval:

                MentorAPM, LLC (Phoenix, AZ) …………………….. $400,000
                             (Implementation Services 13,000
                            Years 1-3 @ 72,000 per year, fixed
                Years 4-5 @ subject to increase of no more than 8% per year)

This purchase is being made in accordance with Section 2-196 (a)(5) of the Procurement Code,
which allows for a sole source procurement to be used for the purchases of supplies, services, or
construction when such supply, services, or construction is available from only one source. The
Procurement and Supply Management Director has reviewed the sole source documents and
determined the purchase of MentorLENS software, implementation, and maintenance shall be
made a sole source. An agreement will be issued for the initial five-year term. The agreement will
have successive renewals based on mutual agreement from both the City and the vendor.

Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funding for implementation services and year one
have been previously appropriated in the General Fund (0001), Public Works Administration
Department, Asset Management Division (040-1019). Funding for future years will be included
in the City’s annual operating budget subject to approval by City Council.

CB-8




       CB-8
                                          ST. PETERSBURG CITY COUNCIL

                                                      Consent Agenda

                                               Meeting of July 23, 2026


TO:                 The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair and Members of City Council

SUBJECT:       A resolution authorizing the Mayor, or his designee, to execute a License
Agreement with the Pinellas Studio of Dance, Inc., a Florida profit corporation, for a
period of thirty-six (36) months to utilize ±6,140 sq. ft. of space within the St. Petersburg
Shuffleboard Club building located at 559 Mirror Lake Drive North, St. Petersburg,
Florida, at a rental rate of $300.00 per month, subject to certain terms and conditions;
and authorizing the execution all documents necessary to effectuate same; waiving the
reserve for replacement requirement of City Council Resolution No. 79-740A; and
providing an effective date. (Requires affirmative vote of at least six (6) members of
City Council.)

EXPLANATION:          The Real Estate and Property Management Department received a
request from the Pinellas Studio of Dance, Inc., a Florida profit corporation ("Licensee"),
to enter into a license agreement to continue its use of the second-floor space (consisting
of ±6,140 sq. ft.) within the St. Petersburg Shuffleboard Club building located at 559
Mirror Lake Drive North, St. Petersburg, Florida ("Premises"). The Licensee has utilized
the Premises since July, 2004.

The proposed License Agreement ("License") is for a term of thirty-six (36) months,
subject to City Council approval, with the terms and conditions providing the Licensee
with the same basic rights and privileges it has enjoyed during the preceding term. The
Licensee will provide instruction, classes and an annual special event for advanced dance
students. The rental rate will be three hundred dollars ($300.00) per month, plus
applicable sales tax during the term. The Licensee is responsible for daily cleaning and
removal of all trash and debris, in addition to providing and paying for all costs (for
certain utilities/services including, but not limited to, telephone, internet service, and
cable/satellite television in association with its use of the Premises. Additionally, the
Licensee will maintain a commercial general liability insurance policy in the amount of
$1,000,000 per occurrence and $2,000,000 in the aggregate, protecting the City against
all claims or demands that may arise or be claimed on account of the Licensee’s use of
the Premises. The License may be terminated without cause by either party with sixty
(60) days written notice prior to the scheduled date of termination. Under the terms of
the License, the City is under no obligation to provide a replacement facility under any
circumstances.

City Council Resolution No. 79-740A, dated October 4, 1979, establishes policies for the
sale and leasing of City-owned park and waterfront property. This resolution requires
that when leasing City property to a non-profit, private organization ". . . the organization
pays operating costs plus a reserve for replacement." The Licensee charges fees to
provide services through the City’s Parks and Recreation programs and has limited
financial resources. Therefore, the City has always charged a nominal fee and




CM 260723 – 1 RE Pinellas Studio of Dance, Inc. (L-4630) 2026-2029                     Page 1
recommends that the reserve for replacement requirement be waived in an effort to
minimize operating costs. These terms and conditions are consistent with prior licenses
with the Licensee with other non-for-profit organizations.

Section 1.02 (c)(2) of the City Charter permits City Council approval of leases and licenses
for park and waterfront property for five (5) years or less on commercially-zoned
property with approval by an affirmative vote of at least six (6) members of City Council.
The subject property is zoned Downtown Center-2.

RECOMMENDATION:            Administration recommends that City Council adopt the
attached resolution authorizing the Mayor, or his designee, to execute a License
Agreement with the Pinellas Studio of Dance, Inc., a Florida profit corporation, for a
period of thirty-six (36) months to utilize ±6,140 sq. ft. of space within the St. Petersburg
Shuffleboard Club building located at 559 Mirror Lake Drive North, St. Petersburg,
Florida, at a rental rate of $300.00 per month, subject to certain terms and conditions;
and authorizing the execution of all documents necessary to effectuate same; waiving
the reserve for replacement requirement of City Council Resolution No. 79-740A; and
providing an effective date.

                                          Consent Agenda

                                      Meeting of July 23, 2026

TO:        The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair and Members of City Council

SUBJECT:        A Resolution authorizing the Mayor or his designee to execute Amendment No. 2 to Task
Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A), amended, to the architect/engineering agreement dated July 15, 2021, as
amended, between the City of St. Petersburg, Florida and Wade Trim, Inc. (“A/E”) for A/E to provide
amended permitting services, construction administration services, construction engineering services and
project close-out services related to the 36” Transmission Main Repairs – Water Crossings Project in an
amount not to exceed $110,972.91; providing that the total Task Order, as amended, shall not exceed
$293,567.91 (ECID Project No. 23157-100; Oracle No. 19720); and providing an effective date.

EXPLANATION:              The City’s transmission main was installed below the bottom of the creek Mullet
Creek in Safety Harbor circa 1930. While there are multiple crossings of both the 36-inch and the 48-
inch watermains, this project is intended to address two specific crossings for the 36-inch watermain which
have become critical due to erosion and site conditions. Over time, erosion exposed the pipe, which is now
resting on the creek bottom, perpendicular to the creek flow. The pipe’s existing location exhibits extensive
erosion of the canal sidewalls and general cross section, placing the pipe and canal stability at risk.

There are two critical crossings of the City’s 36-inch diameter water transmission main along sections of
Mullet Creek in Safety Harbor. The first location is near 2200 Marshall Street, Safety Harbor, Florida
(North crossing) and the second is near 760 Elm Street, Safety Harbor, Florida (South crossing).

This project will vertically adjust the existing 36-inch pipe within the limits of its general location at the
North and South crossings. The existing reinforced concrete pipe will be removed and replaced with a new
36 inch pipeline buried deeper mitigating the risk of the natural canal eroding to the point of risking the
stability of the pipe in the future. Following the pipe construction, rehabilitation will consist of restoring
the channel to its natural and sustainable cross section which is consistent both upstream and downstream
of this location and armoring the channel within the proximity of the canal crossing. Soil will be compacted
prior to placement of a geosynthetic liner, bedding stone, riprap, and seeding as needed.

On July 15, 2021, the City of St. Petersburg, Florida (“City”) and Wade Trim, Inc. (“A/E”) entered into an
architect/engineering agreement for A/E to provide miscellaneous professional services for Miscellaneous
Professional Services for Potable Water, Wastewater and Reclaimed Water projects.

On January 18, 2024, City Council approved Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A) in the amount of
$182,595.00 which provided for professional engineering services including but not limited to project
administration, land surveying of the two creek crossings, environmental services to include jurisdictional
wetland boundary delineation, characterization of habitats and identification of the potential presence of
threatened and protected species, including floral and faunal species. Geotechnical field investigation
services, engineering design, and permitting services. This Amendment included a $24,000 allowance to
be authorized if any unforeseen conditions are experienced while performing the work.
On November 21, 2025, Administration authorized Amendment No. 1 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A)
in the amount of $18,737.56 (from the Allowance) for the A/E to provide professional engineering services
including but not limited to preparing an engineering opinion of probable construction cost and tree removal
permit application.

As the project is within the Permitting phase and entering into the construction phase, this Amendment will
provide construction phase services by the A/E.

Amendment No. 2 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A) in the amount of $110,972.91 shall provide
professional engineering services including but not limited to construction administration services,
construction engineering services, and project close-out services. This Amendment includes a $15,000
allowance to be authorized if any unforeseen conditions are experienced while performing the work.

Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A), Amendment No. 1 and Amendment No. 2 include the following phases
and associated not to exceed costs respectively:

This memorandum is to provide information pursuant to City Council Policy (Resolution 2017-156).

   1.   Summary of Reasons for Selection

        The project involves construction permitting and engineering services during construction for the
        replacement of two segments of the 36-inch transmission main along Mullet Creek in Safety Harbor
        followed by the restoration and stabilization of the creek bed and embankment.

        Wade Trim, Inc. has satisfactorily completed the engineering design for this project. This work is
        a continuation of the previous condition assessment.

        Wade Trim, Inc., Inc. has satisfactorily completed similar work under previous A/E Annual Master
        Agreements in 2012, and is familiar with the City Standards.

        Wade Trim, Inc. has significant experience in the design, permitting and construction phase
        activities of potable drinking water transmission mains.

        This is the second amendment to the first Task Order issued under the 2021 Master Agreement.

Edited: 6/23/2026                                                Page 1 of 1
               AMENDMENT NO. 2 TO TASK ORDER NO. 21-05-WT/W(A)
                36” TRANSMISSION MAIN REPAIRS – WATER CROSSINGS
      MISCELLANEOUS PROFESSIONAL SERVICES FOR POTABLE WATER, WASTEWATER,
                         AND RECLAIMED WATER PROJECTS
                           CITY PROJECT NO. 23157-100


This Amendment No. 2 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A) is made and entered into this __ day
of ________, 2026, pursuant to the ARCHITECT/ENGINEERING AGREEMENT FOR
MISCELLANEOUS PROFESSIONAL SERVICES FOR POTABLE WATER, WASTEWATER, AND
RECLAIMED WATER PROJECTS dated July 15, 2021 (“Agreement”) between Wade Trim, Inc.
(“A/E”), and the City of St. Petersburg, Florida (“City”), and upon execution shall become a part
of the Agreement.

I.     DESCRIPTION OF PROJECT

       The City of St. Petersburg, Florida, receives its potable water supply through two concrete
       pressure pipe water transmission mains. The 36-inch (in) and 48-in diameter water
       transmission mains are both approximately 25 miles (40 kilometer) long and were
       constructed in 1930s and 1960s, respectively. The 36-in diameter water transmission main
       was installed with shallow cover using open cut trenching techniques in the early 1930s.
       This water transmission main crosses multiple creeks and wetlands along the 25-mile
       alignment. Over time, development, channelization, and large storm events have
       contributed to erosion of the creek beds, exposing the 36-in water transmission main and
       eroding the embankments adjacent to the creek crossings, threatening the integrity of the
       pipe. There are two critical crossings of the water transmission main along sections of
       Mullet Creek in Safety Harbor that are in need of evaluation and fortification. The first
       location is near 2200 Marshall Street, Safety Harbor FL 34695. The second location is near
       760 Elm Street, Safety Harbor FL 34695. The construction will involve replacing the 36-in
       water main at both stream crossings with new 36-in pipe buried under the stream to
       eliminate the exposed portions. The work also includes restoring and stabilizing the stream
       banks at these two stream crossings where heavy erosion has occurred over time.

       Amendment No. 1 to the Task Order authorized the use of allowance for additional services
       to Tasks 1, 5, and 6 for the preparation of an engineering cost estimate and City of Safety
       Harbor tree permit application.

       For this Amendment No. 2 to the Task Order, the A/E will provide Construction Phase
       Services as the Engineer of Record in support of the City’s full-time Construction
       Engineering and Inspection (CEI) activities. The project is being constructed by the City’s
       selected Construction Manager at Risk (CMAR), Archer Western Construction, LLC.

II.    SCOPE OF SERVICES

       Task 6 – Permitting Services – Amended Services


00718011 - Final
                           Amendment No. 2 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A)
                                             Page 1 of 7
       A/E has provided and will provide additional permitting services including preparation of an
       application for a City of Safety Harbor Right-of-Way Permit, and an FDEP Environmental
       Resource General Permit with associated correspondence and coordination involved to
       obtain said permits.
       A/E has coordinated with and provided information to the City and Pinellas County to help
       evaluate and determine whether a Pinellas County permit for construction in a floodplain is
       required and whether modeling will be required to acquire said permit. This scope does not
       include a Pinellas County Floodplain permit application, modeling, or other efforts not
       specified herein.
       A/E will have an FWC authorized gopher tortoise agent conduct a gopher tortoise survey
       within 25 feet of the work areas no more than 90 days before the start of construction. In
       addition, a technical memorandum summarizing the survey methodology, results, and
       burrow location map will be prepared and reviewed. This scope does not include a
       relocation permit with the FWC, which would be required if tortoises were found and cannot
       be excluded. It also does not include the fees associated with relocating the tortoises to a
       recipient site.

       Task 7 – Construction Phase Services

       Through the duration of the project schedule, the A/E will perform the following construction
       phase services.

       7.1 Construction Administration Services
           • A/E will attend a pre-construction conference conducted by the CMAR and City of
              St. Petersburg Project Manager.
           • A/E will coordinate with the City’s Construction Services Inspector and Project
              Manager throughout scheduled construction duration.
           • A/E will assign a Construction Manager to provide QA/QC oversight and act as the
              City’s Representative for up to 8 hours per week for a 4-month construction period.
              It is assumed the City will provide full-time inspection services during the
              construction phase. Assumes a 4-month construction duration and that submittals
              review begin at least 1 month prior.
           • A/E will attend one in-person or virtual construction progress meeting every two
              weeks. Meetings will be conducted by the CMAR. The A/E Construction
              Manager/Engineer will attend these meetings to provide input on project status and
              schedule (progress). The meetings will include the City’s Construction Services
              Inspector and other City staff as necessary. Meetings are virtual every two weeks.
           • CMAR will establish construction management software to manage, track, report,
              and store relevant documents between the CMAR, A/E, and City that are produced
              during the construction and closeout phases. A/E will use the CMAR’s software
              unless otherwise specified by the City.
           • A/E will provide general correspondence and office administration services for the
              project that will include project setup, a project management plan, and electronic
              file systems and document control. A/E will provide monthly invoices and reports


00718011 - Final
                           Amendment No. 2 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A)
                                             Page 2 of 7
                   for work completed and will administer the project. A/E will review CMAR’s monthly
                   schedules and look ahead schedules and provide comments when appropriate.
            •      Minor changes to design work will be addressed based on conditions encountered
                   in the field and/or at the direction of the City, including:
                   o Field Orders - Minor variations in the work will be made through field orders
                        issued to the CMAR. Field orders do not involve adjustments to construction
                        contract price nor time for construction and are not considered inconsistent with
                        the Contract Documents.
                   o Change Proposal Requests (CPR) and Work Change Directives (WCD) – When
                        RFIs or construction changes result in contract time or cost adjustments, A/E
                        will review and recommend acceptance or rejection to the City based on
                        negotiations of the CMAR’s proposal, as needed.
                   o Change Orders - Individual CPRs and WCDs will be combined into a change
                        order and provided to the City for review, acceptance and execution.

       7.2 Construction Engineering Services
           • Review up to 25 shop drawings, samples, or submittals using procedures outlined
             in the Contract Documents, including requests for proposed substitutions. A/E will
             coordinate reviews with design engineers for conformance with the design concept
             and compliance with the specified requirements.
           • Logs will be maintained using construction document software to identify and track
             shop drawing submittals, samples, and O&M manuals. Monthly updates of the
             Shop Drawing Log will be provided at progress meetings.
           • Review CMAR-provided inspections or tests required by the construction
              documents for conformance to specified parameters for materials, equipment
              and/or installed work. A/E’s review of CMAR-provided certificates and test data, or
              other approvals information provided by the CMAR does not mean that an
              independent evaluation was performed or validated.
           • Review, log, and respond to the CMAR’s Requests for Information (RFIs) (up to 5
              RFIs) including coordination with design engineers and provide appropriate
              responses. Distribute RFI responses to the CMAR and the City.
           • On a monthly basis, A/E will verify that the CMAR is maintaining a marked-up,
              redline version of the Work installed and will separately maintain a field version of
              marked-up, redline as-built documents. Any observed differences with the CMAR’s
              set will be reconciled regularly (at least monthly) as part of the pay application
              review process. At completion, a set of reconciled redline as-built documents will
              be provided by CMAR for development of record drawings by A/E.
           • A/E will provide periodic site visits by the engineer of record or other design
              professional as appropriate to observe the Work for compliance with the Contract
              Documents and supplement the work of the City construction inspector(s) and A/E’s
              Construction Manager for up to five (5) site visits. After each visit, A/E will create a
              site visit report.
           • Provide routine technical support, which includes providing engineering support to
              resolve routine field issues involving design clarifications or modifications.
           • Review and approve alternative construction methods or materials proposed by the
              CMAR. Substantial and final completion inspections will be facilitated to prepare
00718011 - Final
                                Amendment No. 2 to Task Order No. 21-05-WT/W(A)
                                                  Page 3 of 7
                   punch lists of items requiring completion or correction. Promptly after the CMAR
                   gives notice that the entire work is ready for its intended use, A/E, along with the
                   City and CMAR, will facilitate an inspection for substantial completion, considering
                   any objections of the City and/or A/E, and again, upon final completion. The A/E
                   Construction Manager will reinspect the work once after each initial inspection to
                   confirm any outstanding items are addressed to allow for acceptance of the work
                   and issuances of certificates of substantial completion and final completion.

       7.3 Close-Out Services
           • A/E will document final review of the Project and confirm, as part of final
              documentation, that project requirements of the CMAR are met. The review will
              include operation and maintenance manuals, schedules, guarantees, bonds,
              certificates, and/or other evidence of insurance not previously submitted and
              required by the Contract Documents, certificates of inspection, tests and approvals,
              shop drawings, samples, and other data for the City to accept the project as final.
              During this step, receipt of the CMAR’s final payment application will be verified.
              Limitation of Responsibilities – A/E is not responsible for acts, errors or omissions
              of the CMAR, or their subcontractors, suppliers, or other individuals or entities
              performing or furnishing work for the project. A/E is also not responsible for the
              failure of the CMAR to perform or furnish the work in accordance with the Contract
              Documents.
           • Record drawings will be developed after completion of the construction and final
              survey of the Project by the CMAR. As-built information will be documented by the
              CMAR and reviewed by the City inspector. This information will be marked up by
              the CMAR on the construction drawings to reflect changes during construction and
              will be provided to the A/E to prepare and certify the record drawings.
           • A/E will prepare and submit the Certification of Construction Completion and
              Request for Clearance to Place Permitted PWS Components into Operation to the
              FDEP.
           • Close-out files will be provided at project completion including documents and
              records received throughout the course of the project.

To: The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

Subject: A resolution approving a rescission of an unencumbered appropriation in the City
Facilities Capital Improvement Fund (3031) in the amount of $123,743 from the Infrastructure
TBD FY26 Project (21270); approving a supplemental appropriation in the amount of $123,743
from the increase in the unappropriated balance of the City Facilities Capital Improvement Fund
(3031), resulting from the above rescission, to the Fire Rescue Burn Trailer Project (21742); and
providing an effective date.

Explanation: The initial vendor will replace the concrete pad at the Fire Rescue Drill Grounds.
The supplier will furnish, deliver, and install a Draeger Swede Survival Phase 1 Standard burn
building. The concrete pad will ensure the unit is on a stable, hard surface to ensure safe
operation.

Cost/Funding/Assessment Information: Funding will be available after the approval of a
rescission of an unencumbered appropriation in the City Facilities Capital Improvement Fund
(3031) in the amount of $123,743 from the Infrastructure TBD FY26 Project (21270) and a
supplemental appropriation in the amount of $123,743 from the increase in the unappropriated
balance of the City Facilities Capital Improvement Fund (3031), resulting from the above
recission, to the Fire Rescue Burn Trailer Project (21742).

TO:          The Honorable Lisset Hanewicz, Chair, and Members of City Council

SUBJECT: A resolution authorizing the Mayor or his designee to consent to the Assignment of
the Workforce Housing Bonus Density/Intensity Agreement imposing covenants and restrictions
relating to that certain property known as Tortuga Pointe Apartments located at 10475 Gandy
Boulevard North from Tortuga Pointe LLC, a Delaware limited liability company to Tortuga
Bayside, LLC, a Delaware Limited Liability Company; authorizing the Mayor or his designee to
execute all documents necessary to effectuate this consent; and providing an effective date.

EXPLANATION: The City of St. Petersburg created a Workforce Housing Density/Intensity
Bonus Program (“WFH Program”) by adoption of Ordinance 854-G in 2007 which became
effective January 1, 2008. The WFH Program allows entities that are developing housing to obtain
increased density/intensity on their site in return for agreeing to restrict the additional units so that
they would be affordable to households with incomes at or below 80%, 120%, and 150% of the
area median income (“AMI”) for a period of 30 years from the date of the initial certificate of
occupancy.

On September 29, 2008 ZarCalRes Brighton, LLC recorded a Workforce Housing Bonus
Density/Intensity Agreement (“WFH Agreement”) which allowed them to construct 295 units on
the property located at 10475 Gandy Boulevard North (“Property”) in return for the provision that
93 of those units would be rented in accordance with the WFH Program. The Density Bonus units
are rented as follows: 31 units for households earning 80% of the Area Median Income, 31 units
at 120% AMI and 31 units at 150% AMI.

On October 17, 2017, City Council authorized assignment of the WFH Agreement from ZarCalRes
Brighton, LLC to Tortuga Pointe, LLC in conjunction with the sale of the property to Tortuga
Pointe, LLC (Resolution No. 2017-596).

On February 12, 2021, City Council approved a transfer of Membership Interests from Tortuga
Point Venture, LLC (the 100% membership interest of Tortuga Point, LLC) to HLC Tortuga Point
Venture, LLC (Resolution No. 2021-101). The City is not aware of any non-compliance with the
WFH Agreement to date.

On June 8, 2026, the Housing and Community Development Department was notified by the
ownership group of ARIUM Tortuga Pointe of intention to sell the property to Tortuga Bayside
LLC, as successor-in-interest to Asset Development and Management Group, LLC and request of
Assignment by City Council. The incoming property manager, American Landmark, has
approximately $6 billion gross asset value under management and manages over 36,000 units
across the Sunbelt and is locally based in Tampa, FL.

RECOMMENDATION: Administration recommends that City Council adopt the resolution
authorizing the Mayor or his designee to consent to the Assignment of the Workforce Housing


4919-7975-9285, v.2
Bonus Density/Intensity Agreement imposing covenants and restrictions relating to that certain
property known as Tortuga Pointe Apartments located at 10475 Gandy Boulevard North from
Tortuga Pointe LLC, a Delaware limited liability company to Tortuga Bayside, LLC, a Delaware
Limited Liability Company; authorizing the Mayor or his designee to execute all documents
necessary to effectuate this consent; and providing an effective date.

COST/FUNDING/ASSESSMENT INFORMATION: Not Applicable.

Dear Housing and Community Development Office and Mayor Ken Welch,

Tortuga Pointe LLC is the Owner (“Owner”) under that certain Workforce Housing Bonus
Density/Intensity Agreement (“Agreement”) dated July 18, 2008 between ZarCalRes Brighton,
LLC (predecessor in interest to Owner) and the City of St. Petersburg, Florida (the “City”). The
Agreement covers that certain property known as Tortuga Pointe Apartments located at 10475
Gandy Boulevard, St. Petersburg, Florida (the “Property”).

Pursuant to Section 17 of the Agreement, Owner may not delegate performance under the
Agreement, nor assign the Agreement or any of its rights under the Agreement without the City
Council’s prior written consent which shall be granted or withheld in the City Council’s sole
discretion. The City’s primary goal for requiring such consent is to assure that all the provisions
of the Agreement will be complied with by the Assignee and that the City continues to have the
ability to enforce the Agreement against the Assignee in the simplest and easiest (for the City)
manner possible. Any such purported delegation or assignment without the City’s Council’s
consent shall be null and void and shall constitute a material default of the Agreement.

Section 28 further provides that for purposes of the Agreement any required written permission,
consent, approval or agreement by the City means the approval of the Mayor or the Mayor’s
designee unless otherwise set forth therein.

Owner has entered into a Purchase and Sale Agreement to sell the Property (hereinafter, the
“Sale”) to Tortuga Bayside LLC, as successor-in-interest to Asset Development and
Management Group, LLC (“Buyer”).

Owner hereby notifies the City and further requests the City to consent to, accept and recognize
the assignment, upon a successful Sale, of all Owner’s rights, obligations and liabilities under the
Agreement to Buyer and Buyer, upon a successful Sale, will accept the assignment of all of
Owner’s rights, obligations and liabilities under the Agreement.

Owner has drafted an agreement enclosed herein, which acknowledges that, upon a successful
Sale, the City shall thereafter recognize Buyer as the Owner/Developer under the Agreement and


4919-7975-9285, v.2
release Tortuga Pointe LLC therefrom. Buyer and Owner expect the Sale to occur on or before
July 16, 2026.

We understand that the City will seek City Council’s approval of the assignment and agreement
to the form of assignment which the City, Owner and Buyer will execute as part of the Sale.
Please let us know if you would like to discuss the form of agreement by contacting Matt Moody
at 404-812-8289 or matt.moody@rmrgroup.com.

Should the Sale between Buyer and Owner be terminated, we will notify you.

Sincerely,

Tortuga Pointe LLC,
a Delaware limited liability company

                                Assignment of Agreement

       WHEREAS, the City of St. Petersburg, Florida (the “City”) and ZarCalRes Brighton,
LLC, a Delaware limited liability company (“ZarCal”) previously entered into a Workforce
Housing Bonus Density/Intensity Agreement Imposing Covenants and Restrictions on Real
Property, dated as of July 18, 2008 (the “WFH Agreement”), in connection with the
construction of an apartment complex commonly known as “Tortuga Pointe Apartments”,
located at 10475 Gandy Boulevard North, St. Petersburg, Florida (the “Tortuga Pointe
Apartments”) and legally described on Exhibit “A” attached hereto (“Property”); and

        WHEREAS, the WFH Agreement was recorded in the Pinellas County Clerk of Court’s
official record Book: 16388, Pages 2368-2389 on September 29, 2008, and states that Assignor
may not delegate performance under the WFH Agreement without City Council’s prior written
consent which shall be granted or withheld in the City Council’s sole discretion; and

        WHEREAS, ZarCal requested the City’s consent to the assignment of rights, duties and
obligations under the WFH Agreement to Tortuga Pointe LLC, a Delaware limited liability
company (“Assignor”), in connection with the sale of the Tortuga Pointe Apartments which
occurred on November 7, 2017; and

         WHEREAS, ZarCal entered into an Assignment of Agreement with Assignor assigning
its rights and duties under the WFH Agreement to Assignor, which assignment was consented to
by the City pursuant to Resolution No. 2017-596, and which Assignment of Agreement was
recorded in the Pinellas County Clerk of Court’s Official Record Book 19835, Pages 1204-1210
on November 8, 2017; and

       WHEREAS, Tortuga Pointe Venture LLC, a Delaware limited liability company, the
owner of 100 percent of the membership interests in Assignor (“Membership Interests”)
requested the City’s consent to the sale of Membership Interests constituting a transfer under
Section 14(B) of the WFH Agreement, in connection with the sale of the Membership Interest to
HLC Tortuga Pointe Ventures, LLC, a Delaware limited liability company, which occurred on
March 4, 2021; and

       WHEREAS, the City consented to the transfer of Membership Interests pursuant to
Resolution No. 2021-101, and which Consent to Transfer of Membership Interests was recorded

in the Pinellas County Clerk of Court’s Official Record Book 21431, Pages 342-348 on March
11, 2021; and

       WHEREAS, Assignor, through the Assignment Date (as hereinafter defined), has
successfully performed its duties under the WFH Agreement; and

        WHEREAS, Assignor sent a letter to the City on June ___, 2026 requesting that Assignor
be able to assign its duties under the WFH Agreement in connection with the pending sale of
Tortuga Pointe, which is expected to occur on or after July 23, 2026; and

         WHEREAS, the City was notified on June 8, 2026, that the prospective buyer has formed
an affiliate or related entity, Tortuga Bayside LLC, a Delaware limited liability company
(“Assignee”), to take title to the Property, which entity has been qualified in Florida prior to the
closing of the sale; and

         WHEREAS, Assignee hereby acknowledges that it is fully aware of the requirements and
duties contained in the WFH Agreement and agrees to comply with all provisions of said
Agreement until its expiration on April 8, 2040, which is 30 years from the date on which the
initial Certificate of Occupancy was issued for the Tortuga Pointe Apartments; and

       WHEREAS, on July ____,2026, the City Council, by Resolution Number 2026-___
authorized the Mayor’s designee to approve the Assignment of the WFH Agreement from
Assignor to Assignee; and

       WHEREAS, the City, upon execution of the Consent to Assignment set forth herein,
consents to this Assignment effective of the Assignment Date; and

         WHEREAS, the parties hereto hereby agree that the effective date of this Assignment is
[July ], 2026 (the “Assignment Date”), and that the term “Developer” as used in the WFH
Agreement shall mean Assignee from and after the Assignment Date through the remaining term
of the WFH Agreement; and that all other terms and conditions of the WFH Agreement shall
remain unchanged and are in full force and effect as of the Assignment Date.

        NOW THEREFORE, in consideration of good and valuable consideration, the receipt
and adequacy of which are hereby acknowledged, Tortuga Pointe LLC, as Assignor hereby
transfers and assigns to Tortuga Bayside LLC, its successor and assigns, as Assignee, all of its
rights, duties and interests in and to the WFH Agreement as of the Assignment Date.

       FURTHERMORE, Assignor authorizes and empowers Assignee, on its performance of
the covenants and conditions set forth in the WFH Agreement, to demand and receive of the City
any rights and benefits covenanted to be given in the WFH Agreement hereby assigned, in the
same manner and with the same effect as it could have done had this Assignment not been made.

                                  RESOLUTION NO. 2026-_______

                A RESOLUTION AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR HIS
                DESIGNEE TO CONSENT TO THE ASSIGNMENT OF THE
                WORKFORCE HOUSING BONUS DENSITY/INTENSITY
                AGREEMENT DATED JULY 18, 2008 (AND RECORDED
                SEPTEMBER 29, 2008), AS PREVIOUSLY ASSIGNED,
                IMPOSING COVENANTS AND RESTRICTIONS RELATING TO
                THAT CERTAIN PROPERTY KNOWN AS TORTUGA POINTE
                APARTMENTS, LOCATED AT 10475 GANDY BOULEVARD
                NORTH, FROM TORTUGA POINTE LLC, A DELAWARE
                LIMITED LIABILITY COMPANY, TO TORTUGA BAYSIDE
                LLC, A DELAWARE LIMITED LIABILITY COMPANY;
                AUTHORIZING THE CITY ATTORNEY’S OFFICE TO MAKE
                NON-SUBSTANTIVE CHANGES TO THE ASSIGNMENT OF
                AGREEMENT; AUTHORIZING THE MAYOR OR HIS
                DESIGNEE TO EXECUTE ALL DOCUMENTS NECESSARY TO
                EFFECTUATE THE CONSENT TO THE ASSIGNMENT OF
                AGREEMENT; AND PROVIDING AN EFFECTIVE DATE.


       WHEREAS, the Workforce Housing Bonus Density/Intensity Program (“WFH Program”)
was established by Ord. 854-G on November 28, 2007 and became effective January 1, 2008; and

        WHEREAS, the WFH Program allows a developer/owner the option of building a slightly
higher number of units on their site in return for agreeing to restrict the additional units so that they
would be affordable to households with incomes at or below 80%, 120%, and 150% of the area
median income (“AMI”) for a period of 30 years from the date of the initial certificate of occupancy;
and

        WHEREAS, ZarCalRes Brighton, LLC (“ZarCal”) executed a Workforce Housing Bonus
Density/Intensity Agreement (“WFH Agreement”) on July 18, 2008, which was recorded in the
Official Records of the Pinellas County Clerk of Court in OR Book 16388, pages 2368-2389 on
September 29, 2008; and

        WHEREAS, the WFH Agreement allowed 295 units to be constructed on the property
located at 10475 Gandy Boulevard North (“Property”) in return for the provision that 93 of those
units would be rented in accordance with the WFH Program; and

       WHEREAS, in connection with the sale of the Property to Tortuga Pointe LLC, a Delaware
limited liability company (“Tortuga Pointe LLC”), ZarCal requested the City consent to the
assignment of the WFH Agreement, which request was approved on October 17, 2017, pursuant to




                                              Page 1 of 2
Resolution No. 2017-596, and which assignment of the WFH Agreement was recorded in the Official
Records of the Pinellas County Clerk of Court in OR Book 19835, Pages 1204–1210 on November 8,
2017; and

        WHEREAS, on February 12, 2021, City Council approved the transfer of Membership
Interests from Tortuga Point Venture, LLC (the 100% membership interest of Tortuga Point, LLC) to
HLC Tortuga Point Venture, LLC pursuant to Resolution No. 2021-101, and which consent to
transfer of Membership Interests was recorded in the Official Records of the Pinellas County Clerk
of Court in OR Book 21431, Pages 342-348 on March 11, 2021; and

       WHEREAS, Tortuga Pointe LLC now desires to sell the Property to Tortuga Bayside LLC,
and has requested the City’s consent to the assignment of rights, duties and obligations under the
WFH Agreement; and

       WHEREAS, the City is not aware of any non-compliance with the WFH Agreement to date;
and

       WHEREAS, the Administration recommends that City Council approve this Resolution.

        NOW THEREFORE, BE IT RESOLVED by the City Council of the City of St. Petersburg,
Florida, that the Mayor or his designee is hereby authorized to consent to the assignment of the
Workforce Housing Bonus Density/Intensity Agreement dated July 18, 2008 (and recorded
September 29, 2008), as previously assigned, imposing covenants and restrictions relating to that
certain property known as Tortuga Pointe Apartments, located at 10475 Gandy Boulevard North,
from Tortuga Pointe LLC, a Delaware limited liability company to Tortuga Bayside LLC, a
Delaware limited liability company.

       BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the City Attorney’s Office is authorized to make non-
substantive changes to the Assignment of Agreement.

      BE IT FURTHER RESOLVED that the Mayor or his designee is authorized to execute all
documents necessary to effectuate the consent to the Assignment of Agreement.

       This Resolution shall become effective immediately upon its adoption.

                                      City of St. Petersburg
                     Health, Energy, Resilience, and Sustainability Committee
                                 May 21, 2026 Meeting Minutes
                                       City Hall, Room 100

Present: Committee Chair Brandi Gabbard, Committee Vice-Chair Richie Floyd, Council Member Gina
Driscoll, and Council Member Corey Givens Jr

Absent: None

Also Present: Sustainability and Resilience Director Maeven Rogers, City Administrator Rob Gerdes,
Assistant City Attorney Devon Haggitt, Fleet Management Director Randall Johnston


Staff Liaison: Millicent Battle – City Council Legislative Aide

1) Call to Order – 9:55 AM
2) Approval of Agenda – CM Givens Jr motioned for approval. All voted in favor.
3) Approval of April 16, 2026 Minutes – CM Driscoll motioned for approval. All voted in favor.
4) New Business – May 21, 2026


Presentation and Discussion Regarding the Impact of House Bill 1217 on the Integrated
Sustainability Action Plan (ISAP) – Maeven Rogers, Sustainability and Resilience Director and
Assistant City Attorney Devon Haggitt
        Sustainability and Resilience Director Maeven Rogers explained that House Bill 1217 prohibits
local governments from adopting or implementing net-zero policies, carbon taxes, emissions trading
programs, or related procurement practices using public funds. She noted the bill becomes effective July
1, 2026. Ms. Rogers clarified that there is often public misunderstanding surrounding the concept of “net
zero.” She explained that net zero requires not only reducing emissions, but also actively removing
emissions from the atmosphere through methods such as tree planting or carbon-capture technologies. Ms.
Rogers stated that simply installing solar panels or improving energy efficiency does not constitute net
zero because those measures reduce emissions rather than remove them entirely. She added that the City
of St. Petersburg does not currently invest in carbon-capture systems or operate programs designed to
offset emissions at a net-zero level.
        Assistant City Attorney Devon Haggitt outlined the four primary requirements of the legislation.
She explained that local governments may no longer adopt policies specifically advancing net-zero goals.
She further explained that public funds may not be used to support organizations or procurement practices
intended to advance net-zero policies. Ms. Haggitt stated that the legislation may affect procurement
processes if preferences are given solely based on fuel type or emissions goals but noted that the bill
appears to permit procurement decisions based on broader lifecycle costs and operational efficiency. Ms.
Haggitt stated that the City currently does not participate in emissions trading programs or impose
prohibited environmental fees and therefore does not anticipate impacts under that section of the
legislation. She also noted that the bill requires annual reporting to the Florida Department of Revenue
affirming compliance.
        CM Driscoll asked whether the City would be prohibited from conducting business with vendors
or corporations that independently maintain net-zero policies. Ms. Haggitt responded that she did not


                                                                                                       1
believe the legislation extends that far, explaining that many companies commonly contracted by
governments maintain sustainability or net-zero initiatives. She further stated that the legislation appears
focused on procurement decisions made specifically to advance net-zero policies, rather than prohibiting
contracts with companies that independently hold such goals. Ms. Haggitt acknowledged that the
legislation remains broad and somewhat vague and stated that legal staff would continue monitoring
guidance, attorney general opinions, and developments from other municipalities.
         CM Driscoll also questioned how the legislation may affect City membership and dues paid to
outside organizations. Ms. Haggitt stated that there is currently no centralized database of all memberships
maintained by City departments and acknowledged that renewals and memberships may require additional
review moving forward. CM Driscoll suggested departments should coordinate with legal staff before
renewing or joining organizations to ensure compliance. Ms. Haggitt agreed and clarified that
memberships paid personally by City employees rather than with public funds would likely not be
impacted.
         City Administrator Rob Gerdes informed the committee that administration and legal staff have
already begun reviewing the impacts of multiple recently adopted state laws, including House Bill 1217
and Diversity, Equity, and Inclusion (DEI) legislation. He stated that administrators have been assigned
to analyze each bill individually and identify operational impacts. Administrator Gerdes confirmed that
some memberships have already been identified as potentially affected under the DEI legislation and
stated that future memberships and procurement actions would likely require formal compliance review.
         Ms. Rogers then reviewed the potential impact of the legislation on ISAP. She explained that the
ISAP, adopted in 2019 through Bloomberg American Cities Climate Challenge funding, serves as the
City’s sustainability and resilience blueprint. She noted that after reviewing the document, staff
determined that the term “net zero” appears only once in reference to another study and that the City does
not currently maintain a formal net-zero goal within the ISAP. Ms. Rogers stated that staff believe the
City can continue pursuing greenhouse gas reduction and renewable energy goals because those goals do
not inherently constitute net-zero implementation. She emphasized that sustainability efforts will continue
focusing on measurable outcomes such as energy savings, operational efficiency, infrastructure
improvements, and emissions reductions rather than carbon-removal technologies.
         Committee Chair Gabbard invited Fleet Director Randall Johnston to discuss the legislation’s
potential impact on fleet operations. Mr. Johnston stated that the Fleet Department’s purchasing decisions
have consistently prioritized mission capability, fiscal responsibility, and operational efficiency while also
seeking opportunities to reduce emissions and fuel usage. He explained that the City’s Green Fleet
initiatives are driven by lifecycle cost analyses rather than fuel type preferences. Mr. Johnston stated that
advancements in vehicle engineering and fuel efficiency have contributed to reductions in emissions
overtime and confirmed that the department will continue reviewing the Green Fleet Policy with legal
staff to ensure compliance.
         Chair Gabbard stated that one of her primary goals in bringing the item forward was to provide
transparency for residents concerned that state preemptions might halt environmental progress entirely.
She emphasized the importance of clarifying the nuances of the legislation and reassuring the public that
the City remains committed to environmental stewardship while complying with state law.

                                                                                                            2
         CM Driscoll referenced her prior service on the Pinellas Suncoast Transit Authority Board and
discussed the significant fuel savings achieved through electric buses. She asked whether the City tracks
fuel savings associated with hybrid and electric vehicles in the municipal fleet. Mr. Johnston responded
that the City has improved substantially in its ability to collect and analyze fleet data and now provides
annual Green Fleet reports documenting fuel expenditures, idling costs, and green fleet performance
metrics. He explained that new telematics and GPS technologies have enhanced the City’s ability to
measure operational impacts and savings.
         CM Driscoll requested comparative data demonstrating long-term fuel savings trends and stated
that documenting those savings helps justify continued investments in hybrid and electric vehicles. Ms.
Haggitt responded that the legislation appears to ensure procurement decisions are based on fiscal
responsibility and taxpayer benefit. She stated that if electric or hybrid vehicles provide the most cost-
effective lifecycle option, those purchases would remain consistent with the legislation.
         CM Driscoll stated she was encouraged to hear the City could continue making those investments
and recalled that the Police Department previously had no hybrid vehicles when she first joined City
Council. She noted the City has made considerable progress since that time.
         Committee Vice Chair Richie Floyd stated that after hearing the presentation, he felt more
optimistic regarding the legislation’s actual impacts. He commented that environmentally responsible
decisions are often financially responsible decisions as well and expressed confidence that the City would
successfully navigate future procurement questions.
         CM Givens agreed and stated that the discussion provided important clarity and reassurance. He
expressed optimism that the City could continue exercising local autonomy in procurement decisions
while advancing both environmental and economic goals. CM Givens also voiced concern regarding
continued state preemptions limiting local authority and noted that municipalities increasingly face
restrictions from Tallahassee.
         Chair Gabbard shifted discussion toward renewable energy goals within the ISAP and referenced
questions she had received from residents regarding the City’s 100% clean energy goal by 2035. She asked
whether House Bill 1217 impacts that goal. Ms. Rogers responded that she does not believe the legislation
affects the City’s renewable energy goals because renewable energy production does not itself constitute
net-zero implementation. Ms. Rogers reiterated that net zero specifically involves the removal of
emissions through carbon-capture or offset mechanisms.
         Chair Gabbard summarized that distinction by noting the legislation appears more directly related
to carbon-removal technologies than renewable energy generation itself. She then requested an update on
solar energy use at municipal facilities.
         Ms. Rogers reported that City-owned solar systems currently offset approximately one to two
percent of municipal energy usage, while participation in the Clean Energy Connection Program offsets
an additional six to seven percent. She stated that the City is currently achieving roughly eight to nine
percent renewable energy usage for municipal operations. Ms. Rogers explained that staff are conducting
a solar feasibility study involving approximately twelve municipal buildings and evaluating return-on-
investment projections for additional solar installations. She noted that some facilities show stronger
financial viability than others and stated that staff intend to prioritize projects with the best returns.

                                                                                                        3
         Ms. Rogers also discussed development of a solar operations and maintenance plan to better
monitor infrastructure performance and long-term maintenance needs. She stated that older installations
lack modern monitoring capabilities and that staff is inventorying systems to better evaluate performance.
She further announced that the Sustainability Office recently released its first quarterly solar report
documenting renewable energy production and cost savings, noting that City solar installations generated
approximately $36,000 in savings over the prior four months.
         Chair Gabbard encouraged staff to continue sharing updates with City Council members to support
community outreach and public engagement. She acknowledged that some renewable energy investments
may not always generate immediate financial returns but emphasized the need to balance sustainability
goals with responsible use of taxpayer dollars.
         CM Givens discussed the importance of ongoing conversations with Duke Energy and ensuring
residents have greater opportunities to pursue solar energy adoption. He stated that many residents wish
to install solar systems but cannot afford the upfront costs and noted that state preemptions further limit
local government assistance options. He also expressed interest in learning more about the City’s solar
feasibility study and suggested there may be opportunities for solar development at redevelopment and
brownfield sites throughout the city.
         CM Driscoll raised discussion regarding the committee referral list. She stated that movement on
the Sustainable City Hall Initiative had slowed due to other priorities and suggested integrating the
initiative into the broader ISAP update item to ensure the work continues within the larger sustainability
framework. Vice Chair Floyd agreed with the proposal but requested that the original referral continue to
be reflected administratively so the initiative would not be lost within the broader discussion. Chair
Gabbard suggested incorporating the initiative as a subcategory within the ISAP update while preserving
both referral dates for historical purposes.

      CM Driscoll moved to combine the Sustainable City Hall Initiative referral item with the
ISAP update referral item. The motion passed unanimously.

       Vice Chair Floyd then asked about the status of the municipal electric utility study referral item.
Administrator Gerdes explained that administration plans to present the proposal directly to the full City
Council along with updates regarding Duke Energy franchise discussions and the memorandum of
understanding process. Chair Gabbard stated that while the proposal may move through full City Council
discussion, the referral item should remain on the committee referral list because future study findings
may still require committee review.
       Chair Gabbard announced that the committee’s next meeting would include discussion regarding
a proposed package related to the SunRunner initiative and encouraged interested council members and
residents to participate.

Committee Chair Gabbard adjourned the meeting at 10:48 AM.
